## Genesis 7:11–12 (id: 12957)

* *on the seventeenth day of the second month*: This specific detail gives the flood account a sense of seriousness. It shows that the event is presented as something that happened in history.
* *fountains of the great deep:* See [2:6](https://ref.ly/Gen2:6).
* *the floodgates of the heavens were opened:* The great flood undid the boundaries established on the second and third days of creation ([1:6–13](https://ref.ly/Gen1:6-Gen1:13)). Other parts of the Bible describe God's judgment as undoing creation (see [Jeremiah 4:23–26](https://ref.ly/Jer4:23-Jer4:26); [Amos 7:4](https://ref.ly/Amos7:4)).
* *forty days and forty nights:* See study note on 7:4.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 7:11-12
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 7:4 (ID: `12962@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Noah (ID: `person:Noah`)

## Genesis 7:16 (id: 12958)

*the LORD shut him in:* This showed that God was in control. He protected the people inside the ark and punished those who remained outside (see also [6:16](https://ref.ly/Gen6:16) and study note).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 7:16
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 6:16 (ID: `12946@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 7:17 (id: 12959)

* *the waters rose* (literally *waters multiplied*): This is the same word used for the increase of humans and animals during creation (see [1:22](https://ref.ly/Gen1:22), [28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:28)). Now, ironically, the story uses this word for the water that would destroy them.
* *high above the earth:* The Hebrew word translated as “ground” or “earth” appears eight times in eight verses ([7:17–24](https://ref.ly/Gen7:17-Gen7:24)). The great flood was now cleansing the earth, which humans had polluted.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 7:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Genesis 7:22 (id: 12960)

*every living thing**:* See study note on 6:17.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 7:22
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 6:17 (ID: `12947@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Genesis 8:1 (id: 12969)

* *God remembered:* This key part of the great flood story does not mean God ever forgot Noah. It uses covenant language to show God's faithfulness to his promise to keep his covenant partner safe (see [6:18](https://ref.ly/Gen6:18); [9:15–16](https://ref.ly/Gen9:15-Gen9:16); [Exodus 2:24](https://ref.ly/Exod2:24); [Leviticus 26:42](https://ref.ly/Lev26:42), [45](https://ref.ly/Lev26:45)).
* *wind:* The same Hebrew word is translated as “Spirit” in [Genesis 1:2](https://ref.ly/Gen1:2). This and other similarities suggest that restoring the earth after the great flood was like a new creation (see [Genesis 9:1–2](https://ref.ly/Gen9:1-Gen9:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 8:2 (id: 12973)

*The springs of the deep and the floodgates of the heavens:* See study note on 7:11–12.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:2
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 7:11-12 (ID: `12957@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Genesis 8:4 (id: 12974)

The mountains of Ararat might be in the region of Ararat (Urartu), southeast of the Black Sea near Lake Van. This area borders parts of eastern Turkey, Armenia, and Iran. There is a Mount Ararat (*Agri Dag*) in Turkey, but this verse only identifies the region, not a specific mountain.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ararat (ID: `place:Ararat`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Genesis 8:5 (id: 12975)

*the waters continued to recede:* This is another similarity to the creation week (see [1:9](https://ref.ly/Gen1:9)). This suggests that restoring the earth was like a new creation (see study note on 8:1).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:5
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 8:1 (ID: `12969@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Genesis 8:7 (id: 12976)

The *raven* is the biggest bird in the crow family and in Noah's time the raven was considered ceremonially unclean ([Leviticus 11:15](https://ref.ly/Lev11:15); [Deuteronomy 14:14](https://ref.ly/Deut14:14)). As a scavenger that eats dead animals, it could survive without returning to the boat.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Crow (ID: `fauna:Crow`)

## Genesis 8:11 (id: 12965)

Unlike the raven ([8:7](https://ref.ly/Gen8:7)), the dove eats plants. Olive trees are short, so Noah knew the water was almost gone.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Dove (ID: `fauna:Dove`)

## Genesis 8:13 (id: 12966)

*In Noah’s six hundred and first year, on the first day of the first month:* This was two months after the mountain peaks first appeared ([8:5](https://ref.ly/Gen8:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Covering (ID: `realia:Covering`)

## Genesis 8:14 (id: 12967)

*the earth was fully dry:* This word for dry land is often used with the sea to show God’s rule over both land and sea (see [1:9–10](https://ref.ly/Gen1:9-Gen1:10); [Exodus 14:22](https://ref.ly/Exod14:22), [29](https://ref.ly/Exod14:29); [Psalm 95:5](https://ref.ly/Ps95:5); [Jonah 1:9](https://ref.ly/Jonah1:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:14


## Genesis 8:17 (id: 12968)

*be fruitful and multiply:* See [9:1](https://ref.ly/Gen9:1).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chameleon (ID: `fauna:Chameleon`); Monitor (ID: `fauna:Iguana`); Lizard (ID: `fauna:Lizard`)

## Genesis 8:20 (id: 12970)

* This first mention of an altar in the Bible shows how thankful Noah was for surviving the judgment.
* *offered burnt offerings**:* The same wording is used for the whole burnt offering in Leviticus ([Leviticus 1:3–9](https://ref.ly/Lev1:3-Lev1:9)), but it can refer to any offering that is burned. Noah gave this offering to thank and worship God, who saved him and his family from the great flood.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Genesis 8:21 (id: 12971)

* *smelled the pleasing aroma:* The narrator uses anthropomorphic language (he describes God’s activity in human terms) to show God’s acceptance of Noah’s offering (see also [Exodus 29:18](https://ref.ly/Exod29:18); [Leviticus 1:9](https://ref.ly/Lev1:9); [Numbers 15:3](https://ref.ly/Num15:3)). In the ancient Near East, people often believed that their gods ate the sacrifices offered to them. This idea is not found in the Bible.
* *in his heart:* This is the same phrase as in [6:6](https://ref.ly/Gen6:6). In the same way, here *every inclination of his heart* echoes "every inclination of the thoughts of his heart" ([6:5](https://ref.ly/Gen6:5)). God’s commitment to a new order replaced his grief over the old.
* *Never again will I* *curse … destroy:* The old curse remained ([5:29](https://ref.ly/Gen5:29)), but God promised not to add to it. He set new limits for life in a chaotic world (compare [Isaiah 54:9](https://ref.ly/Isa54:9)). The flood was meant to stop violence, not to change the human heart (the inner thoughts and desires that lead people to do wrong; [Genesis 6:5](https://ref.ly/Gen6:5)). Human beings have a tendency toward evil, but this would be limited to some degree by a new law that holds people responsible for their actions ([9:5–6](https://ref.ly/Gen9:5-Gen9:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pleasing Odor (ID: `keyterm:PleasingOdor`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 8:22 (id: 12972)

God’s promise to sustain the rhythm of the seasons reaffirmed the created order ([1:14](https://ref.ly/Gen1:14); see also [Jeremiah 33:20](https://ref.ly/Jer33:20); [Zechariah 14:7](https://ref.ly/Zech14:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 8:22


## Genesis 9:1 (id: 12982)

*Be fruitful and multiply:* This blessing and command, first given to Adam ([1:28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:28)), God now gives again to Noah. He is the "Adam" of the newly cleansed world in need of repopulation.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Genesis 9:1–7 (id: 12977)

God's first speech after the great flood starts and ends with a blessing ([9:1](https://ref.ly/Gen9:1), [7](https://ref.ly/Gen9:7)). In this speech, God explains again how humans and animals relate to each other, with some changes from the original creation. God gives special attention to the value of life (the importance of protecting human life).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 9:2–3 (id: 12983)

There are two changes to the original order of creation: 

1. Before the flood, humans ruled over the animals ([1:28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:28)). After the flood, animals would fear humans. This uses language often connected with military victory (see [Exodus 23:27–31](https://ref.ly/Exod23:27-Exod23:31), [Deuteronomy 11:25](https://ref.ly/Deut11:25); [31:8](https://ref.ly/Deut31:8)).
2. This fear is connected to a change in human diet. Humans were now allowed to eat animal meat, in addition to grains, fruits, and vegetables ([Genesis 1:29](https://ref.ly/Gen1:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 9:4 (id: 12990)

God gives an important restriction. Blood represents life (it stands for life), so it had to be drained from an animal before its meat could be eaten (see [Leviticus 3:17](https://ref.ly/Lev3:17); [7:26–27](https://ref.ly/Lev7:26-Lev7:27); [17:10–14](https://ref.ly/Lev17:10-Lev17:14); [Deuteronomy 12:16](https://ref.ly/Deut12:16), [23](https://ref.ly/Deut12:23)). 

The Law of Moses also forbade eating animals that died naturally, because their blood had not been drained ([Deuteronomy 14:21](https://ref.ly/Deut14:21)). God provided animal blood to atone for human sin ([Leviticus 17:11](https://ref.ly/Lev17:11); [Hebrews 9:22](https://ref.ly/Heb9:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:4


## Genesis 9:5–6 (id: 12991)

Violence, including murder, was a major factor in bringing about God’s judgment of the flood ([4:8](https://ref.ly/Gen4:8); [6:11](https://ref.ly/Gen6:11), [13](https://ref.ly/Gen6:13)). At this new beginning for human beings, God affirmed the value of human life (that human life is sacred) and established justice for those who take a human life (see also [Psalm 9:12](https://ref.ly/Ps9:12); “Retribution” Theme Note). 

The function of law is to restrain human wickedness and preserve moral order. This law was further developed in the law of Moses ([Exodus 21:12–14](https://ref.ly/Exod21:12-Exod21:14); [Leviticus 24:17–22](https://ref.ly/Lev24:17-Lev24:22); [Numbers 35:16–34](https://ref.ly/Num35:16-Num35:34); [Deuteronomy 17:6–7](https://ref.ly/Deut17:6-Deut17:7); [19:15](https://ref.ly/Deut19:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:5-6
* **Associated Articles:** Retribution (ID: `500155@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 9:6 (id: 12992)

*for in His own image God has made mankind:* God made human beings in his own image. That is why this passage gives a theological reason for the death penalty in the Old Testament. Human beings have a unique place in creation because they bear God’s image ([1:26–28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:26-Gen1:28)). If someone murders another person, that person destroys a life that reflects God in a special way. Because of that, the murderer must receive the highest penalty. See also [Exodus 21:23–25](https://ref.ly/Exod21:23-Exod21:25). 

This does not mean people should take revenge for themselves ([Romans 12:17–19](https://ref.ly/Rom12:17-Rom12:19)). Instead, God has given governing authorities the duty to carry out justice ([Romans 13:1–7](https://ref.ly/Rom13:1-Rom13:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 9:7 (id: 12993)

*be fruitful and multiply; spread out* (literally *swarm and fill*): In contrast to those who would destroy human life ([9:5–6](https://ref.ly/Gen9:5-Gen9:6)), God wants human life to grow and flourish.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:7


## Genesis 9:8–17 (id: 12994)

In God's second speech after the great flood, he promises to protect and preserve creation.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:8-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 9:9–10 (id: 12995)

God had promised this covenant before great flood ([6:18](https://ref.ly/Gen6:18)). It includes not only people, but also the earth and all animals.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 9:11 (id: 12978)

This promise does not prohibit worldwide judgment, but it restricts the means by which God will do it (see [2 Peter 3:4–13](https://ref.ly/2Pet3:4-2Pet3:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Genesis 9:12 (id: 12979)

In the Bible, covenants are frequently confirmed by some sort of sign (for example, [17:11](https://ref.ly/Gen17:11); [Exodus 31:13](https://ref.ly/Exod31:13), [17](https://ref.ly/Exod31:17); [Luke 22:20](https://ref.ly/Luke22:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 9:13–16 (id: 12980)

* God brought great judgment through the rainstorm (a very large and destructive storm). Now he gives the rainbow as a sign of peace for the whole earth (see [9:17](https://ref.ly/Gen9:17)). The rainbow appears after the rainstorm and is connected to it, but it now shows that God will not destroy the earth in this way again.
* The same Hebrew word can mean both “rainbow” and “bow” (a weapon used in war). In some places, God is described as a warrior who shoots arrows to bring judgment (see [Deuteronomy 32:42](https://ref.ly/Deut32:42); [Psalms 7:12](https://ref.ly/Ps7:12); [18:13–14](https://ref.ly/Ps18:13-Ps18:14); [Habakkuk 3:9–11](https://ref.ly/Hab3:9-Hab3:11)). Because of this, some interpreters think the picture in [Genesis 9:13–17](https://ref.ly/Gen9:13-Gen9:17) shows God hanging up his bow. This means he is putting aside his weapon of judgment and choosing peace instead.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:13-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 9:18 (id: 12981)

*Ham was the father of Canaan:* See also [9:20–27](https://ref.ly/Gen9:20-Gen9:27); [10:6–20](https://ref.ly/Gen10:6-Gen10:20). The text repeats this idea to show a connection between Canaan and Ham. It explains that the people of Canaan came from Ham’s family line.

This connection also points back to Ham’s shameful act (a serious dishonor) in [9:20–27](https://ref.ly/Gen9:20-Gen9:27). Because of this, the identity of the Canaanites is closely tied to that event.

The people of Egypt (where Israel lived as slaves) and the people of Canaan (the land Israel later entered) both came from Ham’s descendants ([10:6](https://ref.ly/Gen10:6); see [Leviticus 18:3](https://ref.ly/Lev18:3), [24–26](https://ref.ly/Lev18:24-Lev18:26); [Psalms 105:23](https://ref.ly/Ps105:23), [27](https://ref.ly/Ps105:27); [106:22](https://ref.ly/Ps106:22)). 

Later stories in Genesis describe moral failure (wrong behavior) in both places. Egypt is shown this way ([Genesis 12:10–20](https://ref.ly/Gen12:10-Gen12:20)) and Canaan ([34:1–31](https://ref.ly/Gen34:1-Gen34:31); [38:1–30](https://ref.ly/Gen38:1-Gen38:30); see [9:20–27](https://ref.ly/Gen9:20-Gen9:27) and [10:6–20](https://ref.ly/Gen10:6-Gen10:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Japheth (ID: `person:Japheth`); Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); Ham (ID: `person:Ham`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Canaan (ID: `person:Canaan`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Genesis 9:20–27 (id: 12984)

The story of Noah begins with him walking in righteousness and obeying the Lord ([6:9](https://ref.ly/Gen6:9)). It ends with him drunk and naked in his tent, cursing Canaan. Even after the great flood, people still showed sinful behaviors that led to the flood. 

The text emphasizes the cursed origin of the Canaanites, a corrupt and idol\-worshiping group. Israel would later remove them from the promised land (see also [15:16](https://ref.ly/Gen15:16) and study note; [Leviticus 18:3](https://ref.ly/Lev18:3); [20:23](https://ref.ly/Lev20:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:20-27
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 15:16 (ID: `12339@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Canaan (ID: `person:Canaan`); Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Japheth (ID: `person:Japheth`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Ham (ID: `person:Ham`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`)

## Genesis 9:21 (id: 12985)

*wine … became drunk:* Wine is a gift from God ([Deuteronomy 14:26](https://ref.ly/Deut14:26); [Psalm 104:15](https://ref.ly/Ps104:15); [Isaiah 55:1](https://ref.ly/Isa55:1); see [Luke 22:14–20](https://ref.ly/Luke22:14-Luke22:20); [John 2:1–11](https://ref.ly/John2:1-John2:11)). Scripture clearly teaches that drinking too much alcohol is a dangerous sin ([Proverbs 23:20–21](https://ref.ly/Prov23:20-Prov23:21), [29–35](https://ref.ly/Prov23:29-Prov23:35); [1 Corinthians 6:10](https://ref.ly/1Cor6:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Genesis 9:22 (id: 12986)

* *the father of Canaan:* See study note on 9:18.
* Ham’s behavior was shameful. He looked at his father’s nakedness and did not cover him. Instead, he told his brothers and took away his father’s honor (see [Exodus 21:15](https://ref.ly/Exod21:15), [17](https://ref.ly/Exod21:17); [Lamentations 4:21](https://ref.ly/Lam4:21); [Habakkuk 2:15](https://ref.ly/Hab2:15)).

    An ancient Near Eastern story says that a son is expected to come to his father’s aid when he is drunk (*Tale of Aqhat*; compare [Isaiah 51:17–18](https://ref.ly/Isa51:17-Isa51:18)). Ham did not do his duty to his family. Because of this, Noah blessed Shem and Japheth but cursed Ham ([Genesis 9:24–27](https://ref.ly/Gen9:24-Gen9:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:22
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 9:18 (ID: `12981@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ham (ID: `person:Ham`); Canaan (ID: `person:Canaan`)

## Genesis 9:25 (id: 12987)

*Cursed be Canaan!:* Noah understood that Ham’s actions showed the character of his descendants through Canaan.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Canaan (ID: `person:Canaan`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`)

## Genesis 9:26 (id: 12988)

Noah refers to God as *the LORD*, who made a covenant with Israel. Shem became the ancestor of the people of Israel (see [10:21–32](https://ref.ly/Gen10:21-Gen10:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Canaan (ID: `person:Canaan`); Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Genesis 9:27 (id: 12989)

*may he dwell in the tents of Shem:* Japheth’s descendants would live among Shem’s descendants and share Shem’s prosperity (compare [Romans 11:17–18](https://ref.ly/Rom11:17-Rom11:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 9:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Canaan (ID: `person:Canaan`); Japheth (ID: `person:Japheth`)

## Genesis 10:1 (id: 12249)

*who also had sons after the flood:*This fulfilled the renewed command of creation to populate the earth ([9:1](https://ref.ly/Gen9:1), [9](https://ref.ly/Gen9:9); compare [1:28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); Ham (ID: `person:Ham`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Japheth (ID: `person:Japheth`)

## Genesis 10:1–11.9 (id: 12244)

The fifth account ([10:1](https://ref.ly/Gen10:1)) in Genesis (see study note on 2:4) unites the Table of Nations ([10:2–32](https://ref.ly/Gen10:2-Gen10:32)) and the Babel story ([11:1–9](https://ref.ly/Gen11:1-Gen11:9)) around the theme of scattering the nations ([10:5](https://ref.ly/Gen10:5), [18](https://ref.ly/Gen10:18); [11:4](https://ref.ly/Gen11:4), [8–9](https://ref.ly/Gen11:8-Gen11:9)). The Table of Nations appears before the Babel story, but the Babel event explains how the nations and languages in that list came to exist.

By placing these events in this order, Genesis connects the filling of the earth with the blessing God gave to Noah and his sons (see [9:1](https://ref.ly/Gen9:1) and study note). It shows that the growth of the nations comes from God’s blessing.

This order also prepares for Abram’s call ([12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3)). Abram’s call shows God’s answer to the problem seen at Babel ([11:1–9](https://ref.ly/Gen11:1-Gen11:9)), where humans were separated from God. Through Abram, God begins to restore that relationship.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:1-11:9
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 2:4 (ID: `12412@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Genesis 9:1 (ID: `12982@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Genesis 10:2 (id: 12258)

* The seven sons of Japheth settled in the region of Anatolia (the western plateau lands of Turkey) and spoke Indo\-European languages.
* Gomer was the ancestor of the Cimmerians, who lived north of the Black Sea.
* Magog probably was the ancestor of those who settled in Lydia (see [Ezekiel 38:2](https://ref.ly/Ezek38:2)).
* The descendants of Madai became the Medes of northwest Iran (see [2 Kings 17:6](https://ref.ly/2Kgs17:6); [Jeremiah 51:11](https://ref.ly/Jer51:11); [Daniel 5:28](https://ref.ly/Dan5:28)).
* The descendants of Javan were the Ionian Greeks.
* The descendants of Tubal and Meshech were sometimes allies in battle ([Ezekiel 38:2](https://ref.ly/Ezek38:2)). They probably came from Anatolia's coastal regions (see [Ezekiel 27:13](https://ref.ly/Ezek27:13)).
* The descendants of Tiras possibly became the Thracians living near the Aegean Sea.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Madai (ID: `person:Madai`); Javan (ID: `person:Javan`); Japheth (ID: `person:Japheth`); Tiras (ID: `person:Tiras`); Tubal (ID: `person:Tubal`); Magog (ID: `person:Magog`); Meshech (ID: `person:Meshech.2`); Gomer (ID: `person:Gomer`)

## Genesis 10:2–32 (id: 12250)

* This section explains where the ancient Near Eastern nations came from. Ham was at the center ([10:6–20](https://ref.ly/Gen10:6-Gen10:20))while the descendants of Japheth and Shem spread out to the surrounding regions of Greece, Crete, Asia Minor, Mesopotamia, Madai, the Arabian peninsula, and northeast Africa.

    The list is selective in focusing on nations important to Israel. The 70 names (seven times ten) indicate completeness (see [46:27](https://ref.ly/Gen46:27); [Deuteronomy 32:8](https://ref.ly/Deut32:8)). These names represent the whole world, which Abraham's descendants would later bless ([Genesis 18:18](https://ref.ly/Gen18:18)).

* Although Shem is mentioned first in [10:1](https://ref.ly/Gen10:1), he is addressed last in the Table because of his connection to Abram ([10:21–31](https://ref.ly/Gen10:21-Gen10:31); [11:10–32](https://ref.ly/Gen11:10-Gen11:32); [12:1](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1)). Although God established the boundaries of all nations (see [Deuteronomy 32:8](https://ref.ly/Deut32:8); [Amos 9:7](https://ref.ly/Amos9:7); [Acts 17:26](https://ref.ly/Acts17:26)), Israel was his special creation (a small group of 70 people; [Genesis 46:27](https://ref.ly/Gen46:27)). God called them to be a blessing to a world of 70 nations (see [12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:2-32

## Genesis 10:3 (id: 12260)

* The descendants of Gomer came from the area near the upper Euphrates, north of the Black Sea (see [Ezekiel 38:1–9](https://ref.ly/Ezek38:1-Ezek38:9)).
* The descendants of Ashkenaz were the Scythians, who lived between the Black and Caspian Seas.
* Riphath is near Carchemish.
* The descendants of Togarmah are associated with Til\-garimmu. This is the capital of Kammanu in what is now Armenia (see [Ezekiel 38:6](https://ref.ly/Ezek38:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Riphath (ID: `person:Riphath`); Togarmah (ID: `person:Togarmah`); Ashkenaz (ID: `person:Ashkenaz`); Gomer (ID: `person:Gomer`)

## Genesis 10:4 (id: 12261)

* Elishah is probably Cyprus.
* Tarshishmight be southwest Spain (see study note on Jonah 1:3).
* The Kittites lived in southern Cyprus.
* The Rodanites lived on the island of Rhodes, which later became part of Greece.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:4
* **Associated Articles:** Jonah 1:3 (ID: `19284@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kittim (ID: `group:Kittim`); Javan (ID: `person:Javan`); Elishah (ID: `person:Elishah`); Tarshish (ID: `person:Tarshish`); Dodanim (ID: `group:Dodanim`)

## Genesis 10:5 (id: 12262)

* *maritime peoples … into their territories:* These were seafaring people who settled around the Mediterranean Sea and on different islands.
* *languages:* This happened after the tower of Babel story ([11:1–9](https://ref.ly/Gen11:1-Gen11:9); see study note on 10:1–11:9).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:5
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 10:1-11.9 (ID: `12244@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`)

## Genesis 10:6 (id: 12263)

* The peoples descended from Ham’s four sons (Egyptians, Babylonians, Assyrians, Canaanites) were Israel’s most hostile neighbors.
* Cush might have been in Ethiopia or ancient Nubia (northern Sudan).
* Mizraim was the old name for Egypt ([50:11](https://ref.ly/Gen50:11)).
* Put was in Libya.
* Canaan included southern Syria, Phoenicia, and Palestine west of the Jordan River. In the time of Moses, Egypt and Canaan were part of the same empire. Ham's descendants did not receive the blessing given to Shem's family line ([9:20–28](https://ref.ly/Gen9:20-Gen9:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Put (ID: `person:Put`); Ham (ID: `person:Ham`); Cush (ID: `person:Cush.2`); Canaan (ID: `person:Canaan`); Egypt (ID: `person:Egypt`)

## Genesis 10:7 (id: 12264)

* The sons of Cush and Raamah add up to seven in total.
* Seba was in northern Africa (see [Isaiah 43:3](https://ref.ly/Isa43:3); [45:14](https://ref.ly/Isa45:14)).
* Havilah was in southwest Arabia.
* Sabtah was in southern Arabia, in ancient Hadramaut, near the Persian Gulf.
* Raamah was in southwest Arabia near Najran.
* Sabteca was in ancient Samudake near the Persian Gulf.
* Sheba was a kingdom in southwest Arabia with trading colonies (see [1 Kings 10:1–29](https://ref.ly/1Kgs10:1-1Kgs10:29)).
* Dedan was in northern Arabia.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Raamah (ID: `person:Raamah`); Seba (ID: `person:Seba`); Sheba (ID: `person:Sheba`); Dedan (ID: `person:Dedan`); Sabtah (ID: `person:Sabtah`); Sabteca (ID: `person:Sabteca`); Havilah (ID: `person:Havilah`); Cush (ID: `person:Cush.2`)

## Genesis 10:8–12 (id: 12265)

The text gives special attention to the early history of Babylonia and Assyria. These Mesopotamian empires (kingdoms from the land between the Tigris and Euphrates rivers) later conquered and exiled (forced to leave their land) Israel and Judah.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:8-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nimrod (ID: `person:Nimrod`); Calah (ID: `place:Calah`); Nineveh (ID: `place:Nineveh`); Accad (ID: `place:Accad`); Resen (ID: `place:Resen`); Calneh (ID: `place:Calneh`); Cush (ID: `person:Cush.2`); Erech (ID: `place:Erech`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Shinar (ID: `place:Shinar`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`)

## Genesis 10:9 (id: 12266)

* *Nimrod* became famous through conquest and fear. His empire reached from Babylonia in the south to Assyria in the north ([10:10–12](https://ref.ly/Gen10:10-Gen10:12)).
* *a mighty hunter before the LORD:* Assyrian kings often praised their own power, showing themselves as brave hunter\-conquerors.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nimrod (ID: `person:Nimrod`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 10:10–12 (id: 12245)

* *His kingdom:* This is the region around the Tigris and Euphrates rivers in southern Mesopotamia. This kingdom eventually expanded into northern Mesopotamia (Assyria).
* Babylon is the most important city because it played a key role in the story of the tower of Babel (see [11:4](https://ref.ly/Gen11:4) and study note).
* Erech, known as ancient Uruk, is now Warka in southern Iraq (see [Ezra 4:9–10](https://ref.ly/Ezra4:9-Ezra4:10)).
* Accad, once called Agade, is north of Babylon and was home to the famous ruler Sargon (2370–2295 BC).
* The location of Calneh is uncertain. But it is probably one of Nimrod’s cities north of Aram\-naharaim in southern Mesopotamia (compare [Amos 6:2](https://ref.ly/Amos6:2)).
* *built … Nineveh*: Like Cain, Nimrod built cities (see [Genesis 4:17](https://ref.ly/Gen4:17) and comments). Ninevehwas an ancient Assyrian city on the east bank of the Tigris River in northern Iraq.
* Rehoboth\-Ir was either a nearby city or a daughter\-city of Nineveh.
* Calah is modern Tell Nimrud, south of Nineveh.
* Resen is possibly modern Selamiyeh, northwest of Tell Nimrud.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:10-12
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 11:4 (ID: `12280@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Calah (ID: `place:Calah`); Nineveh (ID: `place:Nineveh`); Accad (ID: `place:Accad`); Resen (ID: `place:Resen`); Calneh (ID: `place:Calneh`); Erech (ID: `place:Erech`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Shinar (ID: `place:Shinar`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`)

## Genesis 10:13–14 (id: 12246)

* *the Ludites* were Lydian tribes located west of the Nile delta.
* The identity of *the Anamites* is unclear. They might have been Egyptians near Cyrene, west of Egypt.
* *the Lehabites* were probably a Libyan tribe.
* *the Naphtuhites* lived in northern Egypt.
* *the Pathrusites* lived in southern Egypt.
* *the Casluhites* might have lived in an Egyptian area also known as "Cyrenaica."
* *the Caphtorites* were Cretans (see [Jeremiah 47:4](https://ref.ly/Jer47:4); [Amos 9:7](https://ref.ly/Amos9:7)).
* *the Philistines* from Crete were warriors and traders who traveled by ship from Crete to settle in southwest Canaan. They lived there during the time when the Israelites left Egypt and in later periods ([Exodus 13:17](https://ref.ly/Exod13:17); [Amos 9:7](https://ref.ly/Amos9:7)). During the early years of Israel's kings, the Philistines were one of Israel's strongest enemies (see 1–2 Samuel).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:13-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pathrusim (ID: `group:Pathrusim`); Anamim (ID: `group:Anamim`); Ludim (ID: `group:Ludim`); Caphtorim (ID: `group:Caphtorim`); Casluhim (ID: `group:Casluhim`); Lehabim (ID: `group:Lehabim`); Philistine (ID: `person:Philistine`); Naphtuhim (ID: `group:Naphtuhim`); Egypt (ID: `person:Egypt`); Lud (ID: `person:Lud`)

## Genesis 10:15–18 (id: 12247)

* Sidon settled in Phoenicia, north of Canaan.
* *the Hittites:* In Genesis, these were a group of cities in Canaan (see [26:34–35](https://ref.ly/Gen26:34-Gen26:35); [27:46](https://ref.ly/Gen27:46); [Ezekiel 16:3](https://ref.ly/Ezek16:3)). They were probably different from the Hittites of Anatolia (Asia Minor). Their empire was a major power in the time of the biblical patriarchs.
* The Jebusites were ancient people living in Jerusalem ([Joshua 15:63](https://ref.ly/Josh15:63); [Judges 19:10–11](https://ref.ly/Judg19:10-Judg19:11); [2 Samuel 5:6–9](https://ref.ly/2Sam5:6-2Sam5:9)).
* The Amorites lived in the mountains of Palestine in Canaan (see [Genesis 15:16](https://ref.ly/Gen15:16); [48:22](https://ref.ly/Gen48:22); [Numbers 13:29](https://ref.ly/Num13:29); [Deuteronomy 3:8](https://ref.ly/Deut3:8); [Joshua 10:5](https://ref.ly/Josh10:5); [Judges 1:35](https://ref.ly/Judg1:35); [10:8](https://ref.ly/Judg10:8); [Ezekiel 16:3](https://ref.ly/Ezek16:3)).
* Little is known about the Girgashites, a Canaanite tribe ([Genesis 15:21](https://ref.ly/Gen15:21); [Deuteronomy 7:1](https://ref.ly/Deut7:1); [Joshua 3:10](https://ref.ly/Josh3:10)).
* The Hivites were a Canaanite people group who did not practice circumcision ([Genesis 34:2](https://ref.ly/Gen34:2), [13–24](https://ref.ly/Gen34:13-Gen34:24); [Joshua 9:1](https://ref.ly/Josh9:1), [7](https://ref.ly/Josh9:7); [11:3](https://ref.ly/Josh11:3); [Judges 3:3](https://ref.ly/Judg3:3); [2 Samuel 24:7](https://ref.ly/2Sam24:7)).
* The Arkites lived in Tell ’Arqa in Lebanon.
* The Sinites formed a city\-state in Phoenicia.
* The Arvadites lived in Ruad in northern Phoenicia, near the El Kebir River. They were known for shipping (compare [Ezekiel 27:8](https://ref.ly/Ezek27:8)).
* The Zemarites lived in Sumur (modern Sumra), north of Arka on the Phoenician coast.
* The Hamathites founded what is now Hama on the Orontes River, the northern border of Canaan (see [Numbers 34:8](https://ref.ly/Num34:8); [Joshua 13:5](https://ref.ly/Josh13:5); [2 Samuel 8:9–10](https://ref.ly/2Sam8:9-2Sam8:10); [1 Kings 8:65](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:65); [2 Kings 14:25–28](https://ref.ly/2Kgs14:25-2Kgs14:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:15-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Girgashites (ID: `group:Girgashites`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Arvadites (ID: `group:Arvadites`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Sinites (ID: `group:Sinites`); Arkites (ID: `group:Arkites`); Hivites (ID: `group:Hivites`); Sidon (ID: `person:Sidon`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); Zemarites (ID: `group:Zemarites`); Jebusites (ID: `group:Jebusites`); Hamathites (ID: `group:Hamathites`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Canaan (ID: `person:Canaan`)

## Genesis 10:19 (id: 12248)

* God set apart *the borders of Canaan* to be taken from its people and given to Israel (see [15:18](https://ref.ly/Gen15:18); [Numbers 34:2–12](https://ref.ly/Num34:2-Num34:12); [Ezekiel 47:15–20](https://ref.ly/Ezek47:15-Ezek47:20); [48:1–28](https://ref.ly/Ezek48:1-Ezek48:28)).
* An ancient highway along the seacoast, the Via Maris, ran from Sidon toward Gerar, connecting Egypt to Mesopotamia.
* Modern Gaza is 18 kilometers (11 miles) northwest of Gerar.
* Sodom and Gomorrah were cities on the southeastern border of the Dead Sea.
* *Admah, and Zeboiim:* The Bible mentions these cities 15 times in connection with Sodom and Gomorrah ([14:2](https://ref.ly/Gen14:2), [8](https://ref.ly/Gen14:8); [Deuteronomy 29:23](https://ref.ly/Deut29:23); see [Hosea 11:8](https://ref.ly/Hos11:8)). God destroyed all four cities to cleanse the land (see [Genesis 19](https://ref.ly/Gen19:1-Gen19:38)).
* Lasha was possibly in the northern area of the Dead Sea.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gomorrah (ID: `place:Gomorrah`); Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`); Gaza (ID: `place:Gaza`); Zeboiim (ID: `place:Zeboiim`); Lasha (ID: `place:Lasha`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Gerar (ID: `place:Gerar`); Admah (ID: `place:Admah`)

## Genesis 10:21 (id: 12251)

* Shem, the older brother of Japheth, was the ancestor of the Semitic peoples (people groups connected by language and culture in the ancient Near East) . The people listed here lived mainly to the east of Israel, in areas like modern Iraq, Iran, and Syria.

    The narrator focuses on places in Mesopotamia (the land between the Tigris and Euphrates rivers). This is important because Abram, the ancestor of Israel, came from this region (see [11:27–32](https://ref.ly/Gen11:27-Gen11:32)).

* Eber receives special attention because he is an ancestor of Abram and is closely connected to the Hebrews (see study note on 10:24).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:21
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 10:24 (ID: `12254@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); Eber (ID: `person:Eber`); Japheth (ID: `person:Japheth`)

## Genesis 10:22 (id: 12252)

* The descendants of Elam lived in what is now southwestern Iran (see [14:1](https://ref.ly/Gen14:1), [9](https://ref.ly/Gen14:9); [Ezra 4:9](https://ref.ly/Ezra4:9); [Isaiah 11:11](https://ref.ly/Isa11:11)).
* The descendants of Asshur became the Assyrians, who lived under Nimrod's rule (see [Genesis 10:11](https://ref.ly/Gen10:11)). Sumerians, who descended from Ham, were expelled by Mesopotamian Semites.
* Arphaxad may have settled northeast of Nineveh. There is more information about his descendants in [11:12–26](https://ref.ly/Gen11:12-Gen11:26).
* Lud was near the Tigris River. The people of Lud were related to the Lydians (see [10:13](https://ref.ly/Gen10:13)).
* Aram was a kingdom of tribes living in the Mesopotamian plains.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); Asshur (ID: `person:Asshur`); Aram (ID: `person:Aram`); Lud (ID: `person:Lud`); Elam (ID: `person:Elam.2`); Arpachshad (ID: `person:Arpachshad`)

## Genesis 10:23 (id: 12253)

* The patriarchs later interacted with the descendants of Aram (see [25:20](https://ref.ly/Gen25:20); [31:20](https://ref.ly/Gen31:20); [Deuteronomy 26:5](https://ref.ly/Deut26:5)).
* Uz was the main Aramean tribe, possibly northeast of the Jordan River. It was Job’s home (see [Job 1:1](https://ref.ly/Job1:1); see also [Lamentations 4:21](https://ref.ly/Lam4:21)).
* Hul is unknown. He might have founded Armenia.
* Gether is unknown. He might have founded the Syrians.
* Mash might have a connection to Mount Masus in northern Mesopotamia or to a part of the Lebanon Mountains.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hul (ID: `person:Hul`); Gether (ID: `person:Gether`); Aram (ID: `person:Aram`); Uz (ID: `person:Uz`); Mash (ID: `person:Mash`)

## Genesis 10:24 (id: 12254)

* Shelah is not well\-known, but it might be short for Methushelah (compare [38:5](https://ref.ly/Gen38:5), [11](https://ref.ly/Gen38:11)).
* Eber was an ancestor of Abram the Hebrew ([11:10–26](https://ref.ly/Gen11:10-Gen11:26)). His name is the source of the word *Hebrew* (see [14:13](https://ref.ly/Gen14:13); [39:14](https://ref.ly/Gen39:14); [40:15](https://ref.ly/Gen40:15); [41:12](https://ref.ly/Gen41:12); [Exodus 2:11](https://ref.ly/Exod2:11); [3:18](https://ref.ly/Exod3:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:24

## Genesis 10:25 (id: 12255)

* *Peleg* means "division," which refers to the dividing of people into different language groups after the tower of Babel event ([11:1–9](https://ref.ly/Gen11:1-Gen11:9)). Peleg’s descendants included Abram (see [11:16–26](https://ref.ly/Gen11:16-Gen11:26)).
* Joktan was the ancestor of the southern Arabian tribes. The Ishmaelite tribes lived in northern Arabia (see [25:13–16](https://ref.ly/Gen25:13-Gen25:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peleg (ID: `person:Peleg`); Eber (ID: `person:Eber`); Joktan (ID: `person:Joktan`)

## Genesis 10:26–29 (id: 12256)

* Almodad was an ancestor, region, or tribe in what is now Yemen.
* Sheleph was a tribe in Yemen.
* Hazarmaveth had a link to Hadramaut in southern Arabia.
* Jerah is unknown but might have a connection to Mount Barach.
* Hadoram was an Arabian tribe.
* Uzal was Sana’a, an ancient capital of Yemen before Islam.
* Diklah was a southern Arabian oasis in Mina.
* Obal was between Hodeida and Sana’a in southwest Arabia.
* Abimael was a Sabaean. These were an ancient people of southern Arabia.
* Sheba was in southern Arabia (see [10:7](https://ref.ly/Gen10:7)).
* Ophir was a region in southern Arabia between Sheba and Havilah. It was famous for gold ([Isaiah 13:12](https://ref.ly/Isa13:12)).
* *Havilah*: See [10:7](https://ref.ly/Gen10:7).
* Jobab might have been Jobebitai in southern Arabia.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 10:26-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joktan (ID: `person:Joktan`); Hadoram (ID: `person:Hadoram.2`); Jerah (ID: `person:Jerah`); Diklah (ID: `person:Diklah`); Uzal (ID: `person:Uzal`); Ophir (ID: `person:Ophir`); Obal (ID: `person:Obal`); Havilah (ID: `person:Havilah.2`); Jobab (ID: `person:Jobab`); Hazarmaveth (ID: `person:Hazarmaveth`); Almodad (ID: `person:Almodad`); Sheleph (ID: `person:Sheleph`); Sheba (ID: `person:Sheba.2`); Abimael (ID: `person:Abimael`)

## Genesis 10:26–32 (id: 12257)

Shem had 14 sons through Eber and Joktan. The tower of Babel story connects Joktan to God's judgment there ([11:1–9](https://ref.ly/Gen11:1-Gen11:9)) and connects Peleg to Abram ([11:27–12:1](https://ref.ly/Gen11:27-Gen12:1)).

## Genesis 10:30 (id: 12259)

* Mesha was a region in northern Arabia, located south of Hadramaut.
* Sephar is the same as Isfar, which is south of Hadramaut in Yemen.

## Genesis 11:1 (id: 12270)

*Now*: The events in [11:1–9](https://ref.ly/Gen11:1-Gen11:9) caused the nations to scatter, as shown in the family histories of [10:2–30](https://ref.ly/Gen10:2-Gen10:30). Although these events happened in a different order than they are written, the author arranged them this way to help explain things about God's actions and plans more clearly (see study note on 10:1–11:9).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:1
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 10:1-11.9 (ID: `12244@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Genesis 11:1–9 (id: 12267)

The story of the unfinished tower continues themes from the Table of Nations (chapter [10](https://ref.ly/Gen10:1-Gen10:32)). These themes include language and human unity. The builders wanted independence from God, which recalls the rebellion in the garden of Eden (chapter [3](https://ref.ly/Gen3:1-Gen3:24)). This also helps show why Abram’s faithful response to God is needed (chapter [12](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:20)) during a time of disorder among the nations.

The scattering of the nations also points forward to later warnings given to Israel. God warned that idolatry would lead to their being scattered, and that their cities would be ruined (see [Leviticus 26:33](https://ref.ly/Lev26:33); [Numbers 10:35](https://ref.ly/Num10:35); [Deuteronomy 4:27](https://ref.ly/Deut4:27); [28:64](https://ref.ly/Deut28:64); [30:3](https://ref.ly/Deut30:3)). 

In the timeline of Genesis, this goes back to an earlier time. It explains how the nations spread out during the time of Peleg (see [Genesis 10:25](https://ref.ly/Gen10:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:1-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Shinar (ID: `place:Shinar`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Brick (ID: `realia:Brick`)

## Genesis 11:2 (id: 12276)

* *journeyed eastward:* See study note on 3:24.
* Shinar (Babylonia) was in southern Mesopotamia, where Nimrod later built his empire and cities (see [10:10](https://ref.ly/Gen10:10); [Isaiah 11:11](https://ref.ly/Isa11:11); [Daniel 1:2](https://ref.ly/Dan1:2); [Zechariah 5:11](https://ref.ly/Zech5:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:2
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 3:24 (ID: `12605@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shinar (ID: `place:Shinar`)

## Genesis 11:3 (id: 12279)

* Stone was plentiful in Canaan. In Mesopotamia, stone was rare, so they developed brick\-making technology.
* They made *tar* from bitumen, a natural, waterproof asphalt similar to cement (see [6:14](https://ref.ly/Gen6:14); [Exodus 2:3](https://ref.ly/Exod2:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Brick (ID: `realia:Brick`)

## Genesis 11:4 (id: 12280)

* Far from the original garden ([2:15](https://ref.ly/Gen2:15)), the first cities of Genesis represent arrogance ([4:17](https://ref.ly/Gen4:17)), tyranny ([10:8–12](https://ref.ly/Gen10:8-Gen10:12)), and evil ([18:20–21](https://ref.ly/Gen18:20-Gen18:21)). The city on the Babylonian plain attracted human pride and idol worship.
* *a tower that reaches to the heavens:* This was probably a temple\-tower (a ziggurat). In ancient Babylonian cities, people considered ziggurats to be sacred mountains where gods came down to earth. Jacob’s dream in [28:12](https://ref.ly/Gen28:12) might indicate this idea.
* *that we may make a name for ourselves:* The tower builders wanted fame through their idol\-worshiping ambition. God promised to make Abram famous because of his humble obedience ([12:2](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`)

## Genesis 11:5 (id: 12281)

*came down:* The tower was a human attempt to reach up to God’s realm (God’s dwelling place; see [Deuteronomy 26:15](https://ref.ly/Deut26:15); [Psalms 2:4](https://ref.ly/Ps2:4); [103:19](https://ref.ly/Ps103:19); [115:16](https://ref.ly/Ps115:16)). This effort was foolish. God had to “come down” to see what they were building, which shows how small and weak their work really was.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`)

## Genesis 11:6 (id: 12282)

If no one stopped them, the tower builders would become united and ambitious. This would allow human evil to grow in ways that cannot be imagined.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 11:7 (id: 12283)

* *Come, let Us go down:* God speaks to his angelic court (see [1:26](https://ref.ly/Gen1:26); [3:22](https://ref.ly/Gen3:22); and study notes).
* *will not understand one another’s speech**:* When people could no longer understand one another, their shared plan to do wrong would stop. Later, at Pentecost, God allowed people to understand many languages. This showed a partial reversal (undoing) of what happened at Babe ([Acts 2:5–13](https://ref.ly/Acts2:5-Acts2:13); see [Zephaniah 3:9](https://ref.ly/Zeph3:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:7
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 1:26 (ID: `12232@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Genesis 11:8 (id: 12284)

*the LORD scattered them:* In a *s*imilar way, God punished Adam and Eve for trying to become independent of his rule. God punished Cain for murder. Their punishments involved exile and scattering ([3:23](https://ref.ly/Gen3:23); [4:12](https://ref.ly/Gen4:12), [14](https://ref.ly/Gen4:14); [9:19](https://ref.ly/Gen9:19); [10:5](https://ref.ly/Gen10:5), [25](https://ref.ly/Gen10:25), [32](https://ref.ly/Gen10:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 11:9 (id: 12285)

* *Babel:* The Babylonians saw their city as the home or gateway of the gods. The double meaning at the end of this story shows Babylon's spiritual confusion.

    Babylon became important under Nimrod ([10:10](https://ref.ly/Gen10:10)) and later in biblical history (see [2 Kings 25:1–30](https://ref.ly/2Kgs25:1-2Kgs25:30)). Its role as a center of pride and idol worship makes it a symbol for the forces opposed to God in the end times (for example, [Revelation 14:8](https://ref.ly/Rev14:8); [16:19](https://ref.ly/Rev16:19); [18:2](https://ref.ly/Rev18:2)).

* The tower builders gathered together in one place to reach up into God’s realm (God’s dwelling place; [Genesis 11:3–4](https://ref.ly/Gen11:3-Gen11:4)). God came down and scattered them across the earth. In this way, he stopped their idolatrous ambition (their desire to act against him and seek their own glory).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`)

## Genesis 11:10 (id: 12268)

* This account of Shem continues from [10:21–32](https://ref.ly/Gen10:21-Gen10:32), focusing on the family line leading to Abram. Only Abram and Israel inherit Shem’s God (see [9:26–27](https://ref.ly/Gen9:26-Gen9:27); [Deuteronomy 32:8–9](https://ref.ly/Deut32:8-Deut32:9)). The Babel story strongly rejects the culture that God called Abram to abandon ([Genesis 12:1](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1); [24:6–7](https://ref.ly/Gen24:6-Gen24:7)). Together with the account of Terah’s descendants ([11:27–32](https://ref.ly/Gen11:27-Gen11:32)), this account of Shem’s line forms a bridge from the universal history of chapters [1–11](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen11:32) to Israel's national history that begins in chapter [12](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:20).

    Abram was part of the small group that remained faithful after the confusion at Babel.

    God chose Abram as an act of grace. Through Abram, God began a new plan. God’s purpose was to bless the whole world, even though the nations had been divided and scattered at Babel ([12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)).

* *the father of* (or *the ancestor of*)*:* Hebrew family records (genealogies, or lists of ancestors) do not always include every generation.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); Arpachshad (ID: `person:Arpachshad`)

*Peleg:* See the study note on 10:25.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:18
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 10:25 (ID: `12255@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peleg (ID: `person:Peleg`); Reu (ID: `person:Reu`)

## Genesis 11:27 (id: 12273)

* *Nahor* was Laban's grandfather. Laban's daughters later married Jacob (chapters [29–31](https://ref.ly/Gen29:1-Gen31:55)).
* *Lot:* See the “Lot” Profile.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:27
* **Associated Articles:** Lot (ID: `500456@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Haran (ID: `person:Haran`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Terah (ID: `person:Terah`); Nahor (ID: `person:Nahor.2`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 11:27–32 (id: 12272)

This brief section provides a complete summary of Terah’s life and accounts for his other sons and their marriages. It also introduces Lot, Abram’s nephew, who later played an important role. The ancestors, including Terah and his family, were idolatrous, worshiping other gods in Mesopotamia ([Joshua 24:2](https://ref.ly/Josh24:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:27-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Haran (ID: `person:Haran`); Terah (ID: `person:Terah`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Ur (ID: `place:Ur`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Haran (ID: `place:Haran`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Nahor (ID: `person:Nahor.2`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Milcah (ID: `person:Milcah`); Iscah (ID: `person:Iscah`)

## Genesis 11:27–25.11 (id: 12271)

*This is the account*: The Hebrew word is *toledoth* (a term that introduces a new section or family history; see study note on 2:4). This section tells about Terah’s family. 

The details in [11:28–25:11](https://ref.ly/Gen11:28-Gen25:11) are about the family line that comes from Terah. The focus is on Abraham and God’s covenant with him.

The passage also highlights Isaac, the promised child. Isaac continued the family line and carried God’s blessing to the next generation.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:27
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 2:4 (ID: `12412@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Rebekah (ID: `person:Rebekah`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`); Chedorlaomer (ID: `person:Chedorlaomer`); Gomorrah (ID: `place:Gomorrah`); Nahor (ID: `person:Nahor.2`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Ephron (ID: `person:Ephron.2`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Mamre (ID: `place:Mamre`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Milcah (ID: `person:Milcah`); Bethuel (ID: `person:Bethuel`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Zoar (ID: `place:Zoar`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Genesis 11:28 (id: 12274)

* Abram received his calling from God in Ur of the Chaldeans ([15:7](https://ref.ly/Gen15:7); [Acts 7:2–4](https://ref.ly/Acts7:2-Acts7:4)). This was the main city of Sumer in Mesopotamia near the Persian Gulf. The family had moved there perhaps generations before this calling. Their original home ("country," [Genesis 12:1](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1)) was probably near Haran, in the region of Shem's descendants ([11:10–26](https://ref.ly/Gen11:10-Gen11:26)). They settled in Haran after leaving Ur ([11:31](https://ref.ly/Gen11:31)). Later, they were described as Arameans ([Deuteronomy 26:5](https://ref.ly/Deut26:5)).
* *native land:* The same Hebrew phrase appears in [12:1](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1) ("country"), indicating that Ur, not Haran, was where Abram received his calling (see [15:7](https://ref.ly/Gen15:7); [Nehemiah 9:7](https://ref.ly/Neh9:7); [Acts 7:2](https://ref.ly/Acts7:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ur (ID: `place:Ur`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Haran (ID: `person:Haran`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Terah (ID: `person:Terah`)

## Genesis 11:29 (id: 12275)

* *Sarai* means "princess" in Hebrew. The text does not mention Sarai's parents. This might have been to create suspense in the story of Abimelech. This story reveals her as Abram's half\-sister ([20:9–12](https://ref.ly/Gen20:9-Gen20:12)). Later, the law forbade such marriages ([Leviticus 18:9](https://ref.ly/Lev18:9); [20:17](https://ref.ly/Lev20:17); [Deuteronomy 27:22](https://ref.ly/Deut27:22)).
* Nahor's wife was Milcah, who was the daughter of Haran and Nahor's niece (see [Genesis 11:29](https://ref.ly/Gen11:29)). Her son Bethuel was Rebekah's father. Rebekah became the wife of Abram's son Isaac ([24:10](https://ref.ly/Gen24:10), [15](https://ref.ly/Gen24:15), [24](https://ref.ly/Gen24:24)). The name *Milcah* is related to the Hebrew word for "queen."

    In Akkadian, it is a title for the goddess Ishtar, the daughter of the moon god. Terah's name is related to the Hebrew word for "moon." His family probably worshiped Sin, the moon god (see [Joshua 24:14](https://ref.ly/Josh24:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Milcah (ID: `person:Milcah`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Iscah (ID: `person:Iscah`); Haran (ID: `person:Haran`); Nahor (ID: `person:Nahor.2`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 11:30 (id: 12277)

Sarai, Rebekah, and Rachel could not have children at first ([25:21](https://ref.ly/Gen25:21); [29:31](https://ref.ly/Gen29:31)). Sarai’s case especially shows the tension between what people could see and what God had promised. God had promised to make Abram into a great nation ([12:2](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2)). 

Israel began through women who could not bear children. This shows that Israel existed because of God’s power and choice. God gave children to women who were barren (unable to have children), as these passages also show (see also [1 Samuel 1:2](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:2); [2:5](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:5); [Psalm 113:9](https://ref.ly/Ps113:9); [Isaiah 54:1](https://ref.ly/Isa54:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`)

## Genesis 11:31 (id: 12278)

* *Terah took:* Abram left Ur because God called him (see study note on 11:28). Yet, the story relates from Terah’s viewpoint, reflecting the ancient Near Eastern culture where the oldest male led the family. This respect for elders is probably why Abram did not go to Canaan alone at that time (see [Acts 7:2–4](https://ref.ly/Acts7:2-Acts7:4)).
* Haran was 885 kilometers (550 miles) northwest of Ur, near the Syrian\-Turkish border. Despite the similar name, there is no connection to Terah’s son Haran, who died in Ur ([11:28](https://ref.ly/Gen11:28)).
* *Haran* means “caravan.” It was a major trade center where ancient trade routes met.
* Haran was also famous for moon worship, which Terah’s family probably practiced (see study note on 11:29).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 11:31
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 11:28 (ID: `12274@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Genesis 11:29 (ID: `12275@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Ur (ID: `place:Ur`); Haran (ID: `place:Haran`); Haran (ID: `person:Haran`); Terah (ID: `person:Terah`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 12:1 (id: 12296)

Abram knew that he should leave, but he did not know where he was going. Obedience required faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 12:1–3 (id: 12286)

These verses are structured around two commands to Abram: *Leave* and *be a blessing* (see study note on 12:2). Each command is followed by three promises conditioned upon obedience.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:1-3
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 12:2 (ID: `12298@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`)

## Genesis 12:1–9 (id: 12287)

* Through Abram's faith and family, God started to restore the blessing. God called Abram from a pagan world to start a new nation. His promises to Abram later became a covenant (chapter [15](https://ref.ly/Gen15:1-Gen15:21)).
* God's calling of Abram later helped convince the Israelites to leave Egypt and go to the land God promised to Abram. It also reminded the Babylonian exiles of their need to return to their own land (for example, [Isaiah 51](https://ref.ly/Isa51:1-Isa51:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:1-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Haran (ID: `place:Haran`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Bethel (ID: `place:Bethel`); Ai (ID: `place:Ai`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); Shechem (ID: `place:Shechem`); Oak of Moreh (ID: `place:Moreh`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Oak (ID: `flora:Oak`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Genesis 12:2 (id: 12298)

* *make your name great:* Abram gained the fame that the builders of the tower of Babel wanted for themselves (see [11:4](https://ref.ly/Gen11:4) and study note).
* *so that you will be a blessing:* This statement is a command and a promise in Hebrew. It depends on Abram obeying God's command ([12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3)): “Go … so that you will be a blessing. I will bless those who bless you and curse those who curse you.”

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:2
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 11:4 (ID: `12280@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Genesis 12:3 (id: 12299)

* Because Abram obeyed the command to be a blessing ([12:2](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2)), God gave him three more promises.
* *those who curse you:* People who ignored Abram and his covenant were rejecting God's choice and plan.
* *all the families on earth will be blessed:* By faith, they could join the covenant God was making with Abram. The blessing spread worldwide through Abraham, Israel, the covenants, the prophets, Scripture, and the Messiah ([Galatians 3:8](https://ref.ly/Gal3:8), [16](https://ref.ly/Gal3:16); compare [Romans 9:4–5](https://ref.ly/Rom9:4-Rom9:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Genesis 12:4 (id: 12301)

Abram was middle\-aged, settled, wealthy, and of high social status. He worshiped many gods (see study note on 11:27–32). When the Lord spoke to him ([12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3)), he obediently left his old ways in Ur to follow God's plan. Because Abram responded in faith, God could confirm his promises ([12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2-Gen12:3)) in a binding covenant ([15:8–21](https://ref.ly/Gen15:8-Gen15:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:4
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 11:27-32 (ID: `12272@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Haran (ID: `place:Haran`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

The *people* (Hebrew *hannepesh*, meaning "the lives") he brought into his household were probably converts. Abram first became a blessing by influencing his household to follow the Lord.

## Genesis 12:6–7 (id: 12303)

* *the Oak of Moreh*: This was probably a Canaanite shrine. Fertile groves were sacred to the Canaanites (compare [Isaiah 1:29](https://ref.ly/Isa1:29)), and *Moreh* means "teacher."

    Abram proclaimed the Lord's name beside a pagan place of worship and instruction ([Genesis 12:8](https://ref.ly/Gen12:8)).

* Abram continued to be a blessing by building an altar to worship God at Shechem and east of Bethel ([12:8](https://ref.ly/Gen12:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:6-7

## Genesis 12:7 (id: 12304)

* *the LORD appeared to Abram* at Shechem ([12:6](https://ref.ly/Gen12:6)) to confirm that this was the promised land. Israel was meant to live in this land, but they needed faith to share in God's promises (compare [Numbers 14](https://ref.ly/Num14:1-Num14:45); [Joshua 1:6–9](https://ref.ly/Josh1:6-Josh1:9)).
* *to your offspring:* Abram did not own the land yet. He lived there as a temporary settler.

Abram needed to distinguish his sacrificial worship from that of the pagan Canaanites.

## Genesis 12:10 (id: 12290)

The Nile River supplied plenty of water for crops, so Egypt was usually the last area to experience famine.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 12:10–13 (id: 12288)

* Abram's scheme came from fear, putting his family and God's promises at risk. He lacked faith when he went to Egypt. He stopped building altars, and deception became the main pattern of his behavior. Deception would trouble his family throughout Genesis ([26:1–11](https://ref.ly/Gen26:1-Gen26:11); [27:1–29](https://ref.ly/Gen27:1-Gen27:29); [29:15–30](https://ref.ly/Gen29:15-Gen29:30); [30:34–36](https://ref.ly/Gen30:34-Gen30:36); [31:6–11](https://ref.ly/Gen31:6-Gen31:11); [37:18–35](https://ref.ly/Gen37:18-Gen37:35); [39:7–20](https://ref.ly/Gen39:7-Gen39:20)).
* Abram's plan probably followed a custom where a brother arranged his sister's marriage (compare [24:29–61](https://ref.ly/Gen24:29-Gen24:61)). Abram may have thought that any possible suitor would have to deal with him, giving him time to leave with Sarai. He did not expect Pharaoh to act without negotiation ([12:14–16](https://ref.ly/Gen12:14-Gen12:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:10-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`)

## Genesis 12:10–20 (id: 12289)

* This story shows that God would allow Abram to put his promises at risk in this episode. Soon after Abram obeyed God’s call, a famine tested his weak faith. God rescued Abram and his family, even though Abram acted foolishly. In Egypt, he used deception instead of trusting God to protect him.

* This story intentionally mirrors Israel's later slavery in Egypt:

    + Because of a famine (compare [Genesis 12:10](https://ref.ly/Gen12:10) with [Genesis 47:13](https://ref.ly/Gen47:13)), Abram and Israel went to Egypt (compare [Genesis 12:10](https://ref.ly/Gen12:10) with [Genesis 47:27](https://ref.ly/Gen47:27)).
        + There was an attempt to kill the males and save the females (compare [Genesis 12:12](https://ref.ly/Gen12:12) with [Exodus 1:22](https://ref.ly/Exod1:22)).
        + God sent plagues to Egypt (compare [Genesis 12:17](https://ref.ly/Gen12:17) with [Exodus 7:14–11:10](https://ref.ly/Exod7:14-Exod11:10)).
        + Abram and Israel took riches from Egypt (compare [Genesis 12:16](https://ref.ly/Gen12:16) with [Exodus 12:35–36](https://ref.ly/Exod12:35-Exod12:36)).
        + Pharaoh expelled them (Hebrew *shalakh*, meaning “send”; compare [Genesis 12:19–20](https://ref.ly/Gen12:19-Gen12:20) with [Exodus 12:31–33](https://ref.ly/Exod12:31-Exod12:33)) and they went to the Negev (compare [Genesis 13:1](https://ref.ly/Gen13:1) with [Numbers 13:17](https://ref.ly/Num13:17), [22](https://ref.ly/Num13:22)).
        Israel was to believe that God would deliver them from bondage in Egypt through the plagues because their ancestor had already been rescued from bondage in Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:10-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.3`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`)

## Genesis 12:13 (id: 12291)

*Please say you are my sister**:* This request appears three times in Genesis (see also [20:2](https://ref.ly/Gen20:2); [26:7](https://ref.ly/Gen26:7)). The text explains that this was Abram’s usual strategy ([20:13](https://ref.ly/Gen20:13)), and his son Isaac did the same thing. The first time this happened was outside the land, and the second time within it (chapter [20](https://ref.ly/Gen20:1-Gen20:18)). This shows that God protected his promise in both regions.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:13


## Genesis 12:14–15 (id: 12292)

Sarai was 65, but she would live to 127\. If the biblical lifespans here are understood literally, she had lived only about half her life. By those standards, she may still have been at an age of full strength and maturity.

Sarai and Abram came from a noble family (see study note on 11:29), so she likely had a regal appearance and wore fine clothing. Pharaoh was drawn to both her beauty and the political advantage of an alliance with her family.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:14-15
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 11:29 (ID: `12275@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.3`)

## Genesis 12:14–16 (id: 12293)

* Abram was bound by the king’s gift to an unwanted agreement about Sarai that he did not want and could not stop. His plan had put him in a dangerous situation. It put Abram, Sarai, and the promise at risk.
* Abram seemed to gain wealth from his deception, but these new possessions also led to new problems. Abram and Lot had to separate (chapter [13](https://ref.ly/Gen13:1-Gen13:18)). Hagar, an Egyptian servant, later became the mother of Ishmael’s descendants (chapter [16](https://ref.ly/Gen16:1-Gen16:16)). In the Bible, these descendants are often shown as being in conflict with Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:14-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.3`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`)

## Genesis 12:15 (id: 12294)

*Pharaoh* was a title for a ruler, not a personal name ([37:36](https://ref.ly/Gen37:36); [Exodus 1:15](https://ref.ly/Exod1:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.3`)

## Genesis 12:17–19 (id: 12295)

God intervened to rescue Sarai and to preserve her marriage to Abram so that the covenant promise could be fulfilled. When Sarai was returned to Abram, Pharaoh rebuked him because of what had happened ([12:18–19](https://ref.ly/Gen12:18-Gen12:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:17-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.3`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`)

## Genesis 12:20 (id: 12297)

Abram did not need to answer Pharaoh’s questions ([12:18–19](https://ref.ly/Gen12:18-Gen12:19)) because Pharaoh’s rebuke was followed by expulsion. Pharaoh’s command echoed God’s command to Abram ([12:1](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1)), but Pharaoh’s words brought Abram shame and disgrace. Even so, God remained faithful and preserved his promise.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 12:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.3`)

## Genesis 13:1–7 (id: 12307)

This story takes place during a time of conflict and God's blessings. At the beginning, Abram returns to a place where he had built an altar. The story emphasizes earlier events as Abram returns to the land ([13:3–4](https://ref.ly/Gen13:3-Gen13:4)). Abram renews his worship and again calls on the Lord's name (see [12:8](https://ref.ly/Gen12:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 13:1-7

## Genesis 13:2 (id: 12314)

Abram already had powerful resources ([12:5](https://ref.ly/Gen12:5)). His time in Egypt increased his wealth and power ([12:16](https://ref.ly/Gen12:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 13:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Genesis 13:5–7 (id: 12315)

* Lot was also wealthy, owning many animals. Tents play an important role in Lot's story ([13:12](https://ref.ly/Gen13:12)).
* The Canaanites and the Perizzites (see [34:30](https://ref.ly/Gen34:30); [Deuteronomy 7:1](https://ref.ly/Deut7:1); [Judges 1:4](https://ref.ly/Judg1:4); [3:5](https://ref.ly/Judg3:5)) controlled the fertile land. The argument between Abram's and Lot's herdsmen made Abram easier to attack.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 13:5-7

## Genesis 13:8 (id: 12317)

Abram wanted to avoid conflict (Hebrew *meribah*) because they were close relatives (literally *brothers*). Later, Moses rebuked Israel for the incident in the wilderness at Meribah ([Exodus 17:1–7](https://ref.ly/Exod17:1-Exod17:7); [Numbers 20:1–13](https://ref.ly/Num20:1-Num20:13)). He taught them to have faith in such situations. So, Meribah became a word of warning for testing and arguing with the Lord in unbelief (see [Psalm 95](https://ref.ly/Ps95:1-Ps95:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 13:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 13:8–13 (id: 12316)

God promised the land to Abram. Abram might have told Lot to find his own place. Abram’s generosity showed his faith. He believed that even if he gave all the land away, God would still provide it to him and his descendants. Abram did not need to hold to things, while Lot's choices were selfish.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 13:8-13

## Genesis 13:10 (id: 12308)

* What attracted Lot would not last. In the garden of the Lord, Adam and Eve gave in to their desire for what they saw. Later, Israel became enslaved in Egypt. Sodom and Gomorrah warn us about putting strong desires ahead of obedience to God ([Genesis 3:5–6](https://ref.ly/Gen3:5-Gen3:6); see [1 John 2:16](https://ref.ly/1John2:16)).
* Zoar was a small town in the plain where Lot and his daughters later fled ([Genesis 19:18–22](https://ref.ly/Gen19:18-Gen19:22)). Earlier, it was called Bela ([14:2](https://ref.ly/Gen14:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 13:10

* *the whole plain of the Jordan* (literally *the circle of the Jordan*): This was probably near the southern end of the Dead Sea, based on descriptions in ancient records that locate cities of the plain. This area is now very desolate.

## Genesis 13:18 (id: 12313)

* Hebron was an Anakite city ([Numbers 13:22](https://ref.ly/Num13:22)) originally called Kiriath\-arba (meaning “city of Arba”). It was located in the forested highlands just north of the Negev ([Genesis 12:9](https://ref.ly/Gen12:9); [Joshua 17:15](https://ref.ly/Josh17:15)). Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob all settled there ([Genesis 18:1](https://ref.ly/Gen18:1); [35:27](https://ref.ly/Gen35:27); [37:14](https://ref.ly/Gen37:14)). Sarah, Abraham, Isaac, Rebekah, Jacob, and Leah were buried in Hebron ([23:19](https://ref.ly/Gen23:19); [35:27–29](https://ref.ly/Gen35:27-Gen35:29); [49:29–32](https://ref.ly/Gen49:29-Gen49:32); [50:13](https://ref.ly/Gen50:13)).
* Mamre was an Amorite ([14:13](https://ref.ly/Gen14:13); [15:16](https://ref.ly/Gen15:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 13:18

## Genesis 14:4 (id: 12331)

King Chedorlaomer seems to have defeated the kings of the Siddim Valley earlier and made them pay tribute in asphalt, olive oil, and copper. In the thirteenth year, they stopped paying. In the fourteenth year ([14:5](https://ref.ly/Gen14:5)), the invading kings returned to bring them under their rule again.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 14:4

## Genesis 14:4–5 (id: 12330)

This was Chedorlaomer’s war. Under the feudal system of tribal affiliations, those in covenant with him had to fight. It was also Abram’s battle to rescue Lot, and those under treaty with him had to accompany him.

## Genesis 14:13 (id: 12320)

* The word *Hebrew* appears here for the first time in the Bible. It is different from the later term *Habiru* found in Egyptian texts. The Habiru were hired soldiers who roamed the land during the time of the judges.
* *Mamre:* See study note on 13:18.
* *a brother*: Or *ally.*

## Genesis 14:14 (id: 12322)

* *Abram … mobilized the 318 trained men born in his household:*This shows that Abram was a strong leader and that God’s promise to make him great was already being fulfilled ([Genesis 12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2-Gen12:3)).
* Dan was a city about 241 kilometers (150 miles) north of Abram’s home in Hebron. At that time, the city was called Laish or Leshem (see [Joshua 19:47](https://ref.ly/Josh19:47) and study note; [Judges 18:29](https://ref.ly/Judg18:29)). Dan, the son whose descendants later settled there during the time of the judges, had not yet been born ([Genesis 30:6](https://ref.ly/Gen30:6); [Judges 18:1–29](https://ref.ly/Judg18:1-Judg18:29)). It seems that an editor later updated the place name so that later audiences would know which city the text meant.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 14:14
* **Associated Articles:** Joshua 19:47 (ID: `19454@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Dan (ID: `place:Dan`)

God could give his people victory over any forces invading the promised land. Faithfulness to God was necessary for victory. God promises to bless his people and give them victory over the world. He uses those who respond to his call and can skillfully use weapons of war.

## Genesis 14:18 (id: 12325)

* The name *Melchizedek* means “king of righteousness.” This suggests the king of Salem was a righteous servant of God. He was probably a Jebusite priest and king. Later writers regarded him as a type of Christ, that is, a person who points forward to Christ ([Psalm 110:4](https://ref.ly/Ps110:4); [Hebrews 7:1–19](https://ref.ly/Heb7:1-Heb7:19)).
* *Salem* is the ancient name for Jerusalem (compare [Psalm 76:2](https://ref.ly/Ps76:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 14:18

Abram understood that accepting the king of Sodom's offer (see study note on 14:1–2) might make him an ally or subject, as Lot had been. This could put God’s promises at risk. Faith looks beyond the world’s riches to the greater blessings that God will give.

In this oath, Abram may have been making it clear that his God, the LORD (Yahweh), is the same as God Most High, whom Melchizedek spoke about. It is possible that Melchizedek did not know the name Yahweh.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 14:22

## Genesis 15:1 (id: 12342)

* *Do not be afraid:* Abram did not have a son to inherit his property. The Lord addressed Abram’s anxiety about the future with comforting words.
* *I am your shield:* The Hebrew word for *shield* (*magen*) comes from the same root word as Melchizedek's word *delivered* ([14:20](https://ref.ly/Gen14:20)). The Lord, who had delivered Abram's enemies into his power, would continue to protect him.
* *your very great reward:* The promise of children was still unfulfilled ([12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2-Gen12:3); see also [Psalm 127:3](https://ref.ly/Ps127:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Word of the Lord (ID: `keyterm:WordOfTheLord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`)

## Genesis 15:1–21 (id: 12334)

God made a covenant (a special agreement) with Abram. In this covenant, God confirmed the promises he had made earlier when he summoned Abram to leave his country ([12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3)). Abram's descendants would experience a long period of slavery before God made these promises come true.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:1-21
