## Genesis 15:2–3 (id: 12343)

Abram used wordplay to show his concern that Eliezer of Damascus (Hebrew *dammeseq*), a man in Abram's household, would become his heir (Hebrew ben\-mesheq*,* which means “son of possession”). This was the usual practice when a man had no son.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Eliezer (ID: `person:Eliezer.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`)

## Genesis 15:4–6 (id: 12344)

God confirmed that the promise to Abram was for his own children. Then God showed Abram the stars as a sign of the many descendants he would have ([22:17](https://ref.ly/Gen22:17); [26:4](https://ref.ly/Gen26:4)). The apostle Paul quotes this promise in [Romans 4:18](https://ref.ly/Rom4:18) to emphasize Abram’s strong faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); Word of the Lord (ID: `keyterm:WordOfTheLord`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`)

## Genesis 15:6 (id: 12345)

* *Abram believed:* God made a covenant with someone who trusted in him. The text does not say when Abram first believed. The Hebrew text does not link Abram's belief to the promise about the stars. It simply says that *Abram believed* *the LORD*. Abram already had faith. Leaving Ur was his first major act that showed it (see [Hebrews 11:8–10](https://ref.ly/Heb11:8-Heb11:10)).
* *it was credited to him as righteousness:* The New Testament quotes this key statement about Abram's faith three times ([Romans 4:3](https://ref.ly/Rom4:3); [Galatians 3:6](https://ref.ly/Gal3:6); [James 2:23](https://ref.ly/Jas2:23)). The New Testament writers use this verse to support the teaching that God makes a person right with himself through trust in him (see also [Romans 4:22–23](https://ref.ly/Rom4:22-Rom4:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 15:7–21 (id: 12346)

In a special ceremony, God made a covenant with Abram and promised to do what he had said.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:7-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Kadmonites (ID: `group:Kadmonites`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Kenizzites (ID: `group:Kenizzites`); Ur (ID: `place:Ur`); Kenites (ID: `group:Kenite`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); Jebusites (ID: `group:Jebusites`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Rephaim (ID: `group:Rephaim`); Girgashites (ID: `group:Girgashites`); Perizzites (ID: `group:Perizzites`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Terror (ID: `keyterm:Terror`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Vulture (ID: `fauna:Vulture`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Dove (ID: `fauna:Dove`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`)

## Genesis 15:10 (id: 12335)

Obeying God's instructions, Abram collected three herd animals for the ceremony and cut them in half. Cutting the animals symbolized the oath. It showed that the person making the covenant risked his own life if he did not keep his promise ([Jeremiah 34:18](https://ref.ly/Jer34:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`)

## Genesis 15:11 (id: 12336)

*birds of prey:* These are ceremonially unclean birds that symbolize those who unjustly attack Abraham's heirs ([15:13–14](https://ref.ly/Gen15:13-Gen15:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Vulture (ID: `fauna:Vulture`)

## Genesis 15:13 (id: 12338)

* *mistreated:* The same word appears in [Exodus 1:11–12](https://ref.ly/Exod1:11-Exod1:12). Egypt, like "birds of prey" ([Genesis 15:11](https://ref.ly/Gen15:11)), tried to destroy Israel and stop the fulfillment of the covenant.
* *four hundred years:* This is probably an approximate number (also [Acts 7:6](https://ref.ly/Acts7:6); compare [Exodus 12:40](https://ref.ly/Exod12:40); [Galatians 3:17](https://ref.ly/Gal3:17)). According to the Hebrew text's timeline, the family moved to Egypt around 1876 BC. The exodus happened around 1446 BC, but many scholars believe the Israelites left Egypt later, around 1270 BC (see Exodus Book Introduction, “The Date of the Exodus”).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 15:13–16 (id: 12337)

Not even 400 years of slavery could interfere with God’s plan to fulfill the covenant.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:13-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 15:16 (id: 12339)

* The reasons for Israel’s bondage included God’s justice. God would tolerate the sins of the Amorites until they fully deserved judgment.
* *is* *not yet complete:* To give the promised land to Israel, God would justly remove those living in the land. Fulfilling promises to Israel also meant judgment for the people in the land. But if they trusted in him, God would save individual people (see [Joshua 2:1–15](https://ref.ly/Josh2:1-Josh2:15); [6:23–25](https://ref.ly/Josh6:23-Josh6:25); [Hebrews 11:31](https://ref.ly/Heb11:31); [James 2:25](https://ref.ly/Jas2:25)). Until then, God would send Jacob's family to Egypt so Israel could become a great nation. Seeing this future frightened Abram ([Genesis 15:12](https://ref.ly/Gen15:12)). But it was comforting to know nothing could stop God's plan.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`)

## Genesis 15:17–18 (id: 12340)

*smoking firepot and a flaming torch:* Fire represented the Lord's cleansing and consuming zeal. It also represented how no one could come near him because of his holiness (compare [Isaiah 6:3–7](https://ref.ly/Isa6:3-Isa6:7)). The holy God made (literally *cut*) a one\-sided covenant with Abram. The promises were absolutely certain. This was because they did not rely on what Abram or his descendants' might do.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:17-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 15:18–19 (id: 12341)

God clearly defined the borders of the promised land (the land he promised to give). He told Abram that even though there would be death and suffering, including slavery, he and his descendants would still receive these promises. God confirmed this with an oath (see [Hebrews 6:13–14](https://ref.ly/Heb6:13-Heb6:14)). Nothing can separate God’s people from his love or stop the fulfillment (the bringing about) of his plans (see [Romans 8:18–39](https://ref.ly/Rom8:18-Rom8:39); [2 Peter 1:3–4](https://ref.ly/2Pet1:3-2Pet1:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 15:18-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Kenizzites (ID: `group:Kenizzites`); Kadmonites (ID: `group:Kadmonites`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kenites (ID: `group:Kenite`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 16:1–3 (id: 12348)

Abram and Sarai dealt with the stress of Sarai not being able to have children and being too old to give birth. 

According to the custom in that culture, a woman unable to have children could give her servant as a secondary wife to her husband. The child from this union became the primary wife's child. They adopted the child as the heir. 

Sarai's suggestion was acceptable according to the culture's custom. But it was a human plan that showed a lack of faith in God. God’s promises would be fulfilled by faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 16:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 16:1–16 (id: 12347)

While they waited for their promised son to be born, Abram and Sarai tried another plan. This plan did not show trust in God.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 16:1-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Beer-lahai-roi (ID: `place:Beer-lahai-roi`); Bered (ID: `place:Bered`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Kadesh-barnea (ID: `place:Kadesh-barnea`); Shur (ID: `place:Shur`); Wild Donkey (ID: `fauna:WildDonkey`)

## Genesis 16:4–6 (id: 12353)

Hagar might have thought Abram would favor her instead of Sarai (compare [Proverbs 30:21–23](https://ref.ly/Prov30:21-Prov30:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 16:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`)

## Genesis 16:7 (id: 12354)

*the angel of the LORD:* The angel of the Lord was the Lord himself in some passages ([16:13](https://ref.ly/Gen16:13); [21:17](https://ref.ly/Gen21:17); [22:11–12](https://ref.ly/Gen22:11-Gen22:12); [31:11–13](https://ref.ly/Gen31:11-Gen31:13); [48:16](https://ref.ly/Gen48:16); [Exodus 3:2](https://ref.ly/Exod3:2); [32:34](https://ref.ly/Exod32:34); [Judges 6:11](https://ref.ly/Judg6:11), [16](https://ref.ly/Judg6:16), [22](https://ref.ly/Judg6:22); [13:22–23](https://ref.ly/Judg13:22-Judg13:23); [Zechariah 3:1–2](https://ref.ly/Zech3:1-Zech3:2)). But in other passages he is distinct from the Lord ([Genesis 24:7](https://ref.ly/Gen24:7); [2 Samuel 24:16](https://ref.ly/2Sam24:16); [Zechariah 1:12](https://ref.ly/Zech1:12)). 

This figure is often understood as a *theophany* (a visible appearance of God) or a *Christophany* (an appearance of the Messiah before he became human (see [Genesis 18:1–2](https://ref.ly/Gen18:1-Gen18:2); [19:1](https://ref.ly/Gen19:1); [Numbers 22:22](https://ref.ly/Num22:22); [Judges 2:1–4](https://ref.ly/Judg2:1-Judg2:4); [5:23](https://ref.ly/Judg5:23); [Zechariah 12:8](https://ref.ly/Zech12:8)). In these cases, he speaks with the authority of the Lord himself.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 16:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Shur (ID: `place:Shur`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Genesis 16:8–12 (id: 12355)

The angel asked Hagar questions that helped her speak openly to God. When she did, God told her to return and submit to Sarai ([16:9](https://ref.ly/Gen16:9)). He also promised that her son would have many descendants, so many that they could not be counted.

The angel of the Lord did not call Hagar Abram’s wife. Instead, he called her Sarai’s servant. Hagar would bear Abram’s child, but Ishmael was not part of God’s covenant with Abram.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 16:8-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Wild Donkey (ID: `fauna:WildDonkey`)

## Genesis 16:10–12 (id: 12349)

Hagar’s son would become the father of a large nation. This nation would be strong but often hostile, living in the Arabian Desert and frequently in conflict with Israel (compare [25:18](https://ref.ly/Gen25:18)). 

God blessed Ishmael as Abram's descendant. But God did not choose Ishmael to continue the covenant. God kept that blessing for Abram's heir based on God's promise.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 16:10-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Wild Donkey (ID: `fauna:WildDonkey`)

## Genesis 16:11 (id: 12350)

Names in Genesis often emphasize the message of a passage. They help the people of God remember the events and their importance in the history of faith. The name *Ishmael* means "God hears." It marks the moment when *the LORD … heard* Hagar's cry of distress (see also study note on 16:14–15). This name would have greatly comforted Hagar. God listened to her prayers and acknowledged her complaint.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 16:11
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 16:14-15 (ID: `12352@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Genesis 16:13 (id: 12351)

* Hagar showed faith in God through her words and obedience.
* *the God who sees me:* God understood Hagar's situation and protected her.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 16:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 16:14–15 (id: 12352)

The name *Beer\-lahai\-roi* means “well of the Living One who sees me.” This name and *Ishmael* were a message and a rebuke for Abram and Sarai (see [16:11](https://ref.ly/Gen16:11)). God notices suffering and listens to those crying out in need. 

Sarai and Abram should have prayed instead of trying to fulfill the promise themselves by following a social custom (compare [25:21](https://ref.ly/Gen25:21)). Giving children to a woman who cannot have them is God's work ([Psalm 113:9](https://ref.ly/Ps113:9); compare [1 Samuel 1:1–28](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:1-1Sam1:28); [Luke 1:1–25](https://ref.ly/Luke1:1-Luke1:25)). Humans cannot solve impossible problems. The Lord hears those who suffer, sees their needs, and will provide for them in miraculous ways.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 16:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`); Beer-lahai-roi (ID: `place:Beer-lahai-roi`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Bered (ID: `place:Bered`); Kadesh-barnea (ID: `place:Kadesh-barnea`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 17:1 (id: 12361)

* *God Almighty* (Hebrew *El\-Shaddai*): This name for God emphasizes his power (see also [28:3](https://ref.ly/Gen28:3); [35:11](https://ref.ly/Gen35:11); [43:14](https://ref.ly/Gen43:14); [48:3](https://ref.ly/Gen48:3); [49:25](https://ref.ly/Gen49:25)).
* *Walk before Me and be blameless:* To bless the nations, Abram needed to obey. Almighty God would guide his actions.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 17:1–27 (id: 12356)

God gave Abram's family signs that his promises would come true. He changed Abram's name to Abraham ([17:1–8](https://ref.ly/Gen17:1-Gen17:8)). He established circumcision as the sign of the covenant ([17:9–14](https://ref.ly/Gen17:9-Gen17:14), [23–27](https://ref.ly/Gen17:23-Gen17:27)). He changed Sarai's name to Sarah ([17:15–22](https://ref.ly/Gen17:15-Gen17:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:1-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`); Foreskin (ID: `keyterm:Foreskin`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Uncircumcised Person (ID: `keyterm:UncircumcisedPerson`)

## Genesis 17:4–5 (id: 12364)

God guaranteed his promise by changing Abram’s name. *Abram* means “exalted father.” This referred to his father Terah and his noble lineage ([11:27](https://ref.ly/Gen11:27)). His new name, *Abraham* (Hebrew *’ab hamon*), means "father of a multitude." This was a wordplay on the promise of his countless descendants (see also [John 8:31–59](https://ref.ly/John8:31-John8:59); [Romans 4:16–17](https://ref.ly/Rom4:16-Rom4:17); [Galatians 3:7](https://ref.ly/Gal3:7), [15–19](https://ref.ly/Gal3:15-Gal3:19), [29](https://ref.ly/Gal3:29)). Whenever the new name was used, he and his household would remember that a multitude of nations would come from him.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Genesis 17:6 (id: 12365)

*kings will descend from you:* This is the first sign that Israel would become a nation ruled by kings (see also [35:11](https://ref.ly/Gen35:11); [36:31](https://ref.ly/Gen36:31); [Numbers 24:7](https://ref.ly/Num24:7); [Deuteronomy 17:14–18](https://ref.ly/Deut17:14-Deut17:18); [28:36](https://ref.ly/Deut28:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:6


## Genesis 17:7–8 (id: 12366)

The land of Canaan was to be an everlasting possession for the descendants of Abraham. The Lord would always be their God (see [Jeremiah 31:31–40](https://ref.ly/Jer31:31-Jer31:40); [Zechariah 8:8](https://ref.ly/Zech8:8); [Luke 1:68–79](https://ref.ly/Luke1:68-Luke1:79); [Revelation 21:1–4](https://ref.ly/Rev21:1-Rev21:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Genesis 17:9–14 (id: 12367)

God gave circumcision as a confirming sign that reminded all households of loyalty to the covenant.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:9-14

## Genesis 17:14 (id: 12357)

*will be cut off:* This punishment seems to have several applications. A person could be exiled from society or put to death by the community; most often it warned that a person might die prematurely (see [Exodus 31:14](https://ref.ly/Exod31:14); [Leviticus 7:20–27](https://ref.ly/Lev7:20-Lev7:27); [17:3–4](https://ref.ly/Lev17:3-Lev17:4); [20:17–18](https://ref.ly/Lev20:17-Lev20:18); [23:28–29](https://ref.ly/Lev23:28-Lev23:29); [Numbers 15:30–31](https://ref.ly/Num15:30-Num15:31); see also [Psalm 31:22](https://ref.ly/Ps31:22); [Ezekiel 21:4](https://ref.ly/Ezek21:4); [Romans 9:3](https://ref.ly/Rom9:3); [11:22](https://ref.ly/Rom11:22)). 

Failure to be circumcised was a serious violation of the covenant (see [Exodus 4:24–26](https://ref.ly/Exod4:24-Exod4:26); compare [Galatians 5:2–4](https://ref.ly/Gal5:2-Gal5:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Uncircumcised Person (ID: `keyterm:UncircumcisedPerson`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Foreskin (ID: `keyterm:Foreskin`); Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`)

## Genesis 17:15–16 (id: 12358)

Sarah’s new name marked an important step in her calling (God’s purpose for her life). It fit her role as the future mother of kings and pointed to God’s promise.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:15-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 17:17–18 (id: 12359)

*he laughed* (Hebrew *yitskhaq*): This was because the promise seemed unbelievable. He thought his family line would continue through Ishmael. But Abraham and Sarah would have their own son.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:17-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`)

The name *Isaac* (Hebrew *yitskhaq*) means “he laughs.” This recalls Abraham's disbelief when he heard God's promise. It also reminds us of God's favor and pleasure at Isaac's birth (compare [21:6](https://ref.ly/Gen21:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:19

## Genesis 17:20–21 (id: 12362)

God would not abandon Ishmael. His family would prosper (see [25:13–16](https://ref.ly/Gen25:13-Gen25:16)). But the covenant promises were for Isaac.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 17:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Genesis 17:23–27 (id: 12363)

After God spoke to Abraham about Isaac, Abraham followed God's instructions immediately. He performed circumcision as a sign of faith, showing their participation in the covenant (compare [Romans 4:11–12](https://ref.ly/Rom4:11-Rom4:12); [Galatians 5:2–6](https://ref.ly/Gal5:2-Gal5:6), [11](https://ref.ly/Gal5:11); [6:15](https://ref.ly/Gal6:15); [Philippians 3:2–3](https://ref.ly/Phil3:2-Phil3:3); [Colossians 2:11–12](https://ref.ly/Col2:11-Col2:12); [1 Peter 3:21](https://ref.ly/1Pet3:21)).

## Genesis 18:1–15 (id: 12368)

The Lord's visit to Abraham set the time for Isaac's birth. The three visitors were probably the Lord and two angels (see study note on 16:7). Abraham welcomed the visitors peacefully and generously, which contrasted with the chaos and corruption in Sodom (chapter [19](https://ref.ly/Gen19:1-Gen19:38)). 

Sharing a meal was important for making or confirming covenants. When God was ready to fulfill the covenant promise, he personally shared a meal with Abraham. Fellowship with God has always included a communal meal (see [Exodus 24:9–11](https://ref.ly/Exod24:9-Exod24:11); [Matthew 26:17–30](https://ref.ly/Matt26:17-Matt26:30) *//* [Luke 22:7–38](https://ref.ly/Luke22:7-Luke22:38); [Acts 2:42](https://ref.ly/Acts2:42); [1 Corinthians 11:20–34](https://ref.ly/1Cor11:20-1Cor11:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:1-15
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 16:7 (ID: `12354@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Mamre (ID: `place:Mamre`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Seah (ID: `realia:Seah`); Oak (ID: `flora:Oak`)

## Genesis 18:2–8 (id: 12374)

Abraham received his visitors as very important guests, perhaps realizing that they were messengers from God.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:2-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Seah (ID: `realia:Seah`)

## Genesis 18:3 (id: 12378)

*My lord:* The Hebrew text uses *’adonay*, which always refers to God. In Hebrew tradition, people said this word when they saw the letters of God's holy name ("Yahweh") in the text. The text here might use *’adonay* instead of the more common *’adoni* to show this was the angel of the Lord, meaning the Lord himself (see study note on 16:7). We do not know if Abraham knew who his visitors were at first. But by the end of the story, he knew he had been talking with God.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:3
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 16:7 (ID: `12354@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`)

## Genesis 18:9 (id: 12379)

The visitors asked about Sarah by name, even though no one had told them who she was.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`)

## Genesis 18:10 (id: 12369)

*I will surely return:* The Hebrew phrase is emphatic. The statement announced a certain promise and dramatic change.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Genesis 18:13–15 (id: 12370)

Sarah thought no one noticed her disbelieving laughter. But God knows what people do in secret and what they feel (see [Psalm 69:5](https://ref.ly/Ps69:5); [Proverbs 20:27](https://ref.ly/Prov20:27); [Mark 4:22](https://ref.ly/Mark4:22); [Luke 8:43–48](https://ref.ly/Luke8:43-Luke8:48); [Hebrews 4:13](https://ref.ly/Heb4:13)). He knows if they doubt promises or trust in him (see [Hebrews 11:11–12](https://ref.ly/Heb11:11-Heb11:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:13-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Genesis 18:14 (id: 12371)

*Is* *anything too difficult for the LORD?:*This question does not expect an answer. It teaches that nothing is too difficult for God.

God can do great and surprising things. For those who live in covenant (a binding promise relationship) with him, nothing is impossible, because nothing is too hard for the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`)

## Genesis 18:16–33 (id: 12372)

God trusted Abraham as his prophet ([18:16–21](https://ref.ly/Gen18:16-Gen18:21); see [20:7](https://ref.ly/Gen20:7)). In return, Abraham pleaded that God would save Sodom ([18:23–32](https://ref.ly/Gen18:23-Gen18:32); see [Hebrews 7:23–26](https://ref.ly/Heb7:23-Heb7:26)). God can do anything he wants. This passage confirms it will be fair and right.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:16-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Gomorrah (ID: `place:Gomorrah`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Genesis 18:17–19 (id: 12373)

Abraham was responsible for teaching his descendants to be righteous and act justly so they might enjoy God's blessings. It was important for Abraham to know how God’s righteousness was at work in judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:17-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Genesis 18:20 (id: 12376)

*The outcry:* See [Ezekiel 16:49–50](https://ref.ly/Ezek16:49-Ezek16:50).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gomorrah (ID: `place:Gomorrah`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 18:20–21 (id: 12375)

God, who knows everything, was cautious in his judgment. He knew the sins of Sodom and Gomorrah. This careful examination showed God's justice in a way humans could understand. He would not destroy the people unless he was certain they were wicked.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gomorrah (ID: `place:Gomorrah`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 18:22–33 (id: 12377)

Abraham probably thought there were more righteous people in Sodom and Gomorrah than there were (see study note on 19:14). Concerned for them, he made a legal appeal to the Lord based on God's justice. His prayer might seem bold, as if he were negotiating with God. But he approached God with true humility and respect. He did not try to convince God to act against his will. Instead, he prayed for the well\-being of others (compare with Lot’s prayer, [19:18–23](https://ref.ly/Gen19:18-Gen19:23)). 

God is a fair judge. "Righteousness exalts a nation" ([Proverbs 14:34](https://ref.ly/Prov14:34)). Righteous people help preserve society (compare [Matthew 5:13](https://ref.ly/Matt5:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 18:22-33
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 19:14 (ID: `12382@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Genesis 19:1 (id: 12385)

* The two angels also first appeared to Abraham as men ([18:2](https://ref.ly/Gen18:2)).
* Lot was no longer living in tents near Sodom ([13:12](https://ref.ly/Gen13:12)). He had become a citizen and leader in Sodom, sitting at the city entrance. Community leaders (elders) usually gathered at the gates. This is where they publicly finalized legal and business matters (compare [23:18](https://ref.ly/Gen23:18); [Job 29:7](https://ref.ly/Job29:7), [12–17](https://ref.ly/Job29:12-Job29:17)).

    As a righteous man, Lot tried to guide the people of Sodom to live better lives ([2 Peter 2:7–8](https://ref.ly/2Pet2:7-2Pet2:8); compare [Genesis 19:9](https://ref.ly/Gen19:9)). While he opposed their evil behavior, Lot still chose Sodom’s wealthy lifestyle over the simpler life in the hills (compare [13:10–11](https://ref.ly/Gen13:10-Gen13:11)). Life in the hills offered fewer temptations and less corruption but lacked the comforts of Sodom.

    As long as the Lord left Lot and his family alone in Sodom, he lived comfortably and kept his belief in God. But, he could not continue living this way. Sodom would have destroyed Lot if the Lord had not destroyed Sodom.

* This story showed Israel that God is the righteous judge of the whole earth ([18:25](https://ref.ly/Gen18:25)). He punishes evil fairly. In evil societies, wrongdoing leads to unfair treatment and suffering for others.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Genesis 19:1–14 (id: 12380)

The two angels who were with the Lord at Mamre (compare [18:2](https://ref.ly/Gen18:2), [22](https://ref.ly/Gen18:22)) went on to Sodom. They knew the people there were very wicked. Even though Lot welcomed them, they first chose to stay in the public square instead of going into his house.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:1-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); House (ID: `realia:House`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Angels (ID: `group:Angels`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Unleavened Bread (ID: `realia:UnleavenedBread`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`)

## Genesis 19:1–38 (id: 12381)

The Canaanites were evil people who tried to corrupt others. God judged their immoral civilization and warned others not to be like them. It was difficult to get Lot and his family out of Sodom. But it was even more difficult to remove Sodom's influence from them. 

This chapter helped later Israelites understand the moral and spiritual dangers of the people living in and around the promised land. This included the Canaanites and Lot’s descendants, the Moabites and the Ammonites (see [Numbers 22–25](https://ref.ly/Num22:1-Num25:18); [Deuteronomy 23:3–6](https://ref.ly/Deut23:3-Deut23:6); [Joshua 24:9](https://ref.ly/Josh24:9); [Judges 10:7–9](https://ref.ly/Judg10:7-Judg10:9); [11:4–5](https://ref.ly/Judg11:4-Judg11:5); [1 Samuel 10:27](https://ref.ly/1Sam10:27); [1 Kings 11:1–3](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:1-1Kgs11:3); [2 Kings 24:2](https://ref.ly/2Kgs24:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:1-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Zoar (ID: `place:Zoar`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Gomorrah (ID: `place:Gomorrah`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Ammon (ID: `place:Ammon`); Ben-ammi (ID: `person:Ben-ammi`); Ammonites (ID: `group:Ammon`); Angels (ID: `group:Angels`); Moab (ID: `person:Moab`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Kiln (ID: `realia:Kiln`); Unleavened Bread (ID: `realia:UnleavenedBread`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`)

## Genesis 19:4–5 (id: 12392)

The evil actions of the men of the city were matched by the hypocrisy of Lot ([19:6–9](https://ref.ly/Gen19:6-Gen19:9)). The men acted in a wicked and violent way toward Lot’s visitors. Lot tried to protect his guests. However, he offered his daughters to the crowd instead. This action showed serious moral failure.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 19:6–9 (id: 12393)

Lot strongly opposed the men’s plan to harm his guests. He spoke against their wicked actions. However, he wrongly offered his daughters to protect the visitors, which showed a serious moral failure.

At first, Lot had only set up his tent near Sodom. Over time, he became closely tied to the city, and its influence shaped his life.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:6-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`)

## Genesis 19:9 (id: 12394)

The men of the city became enraged because Lot tried to stop their evil behavior. It was amazing to them that Lot would judge them, since he apparently had not done so before.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`)

## Genesis 19:14 (id: 12382)

Lot’s warning words were not taken seriously because of his hypocrisy. It seemed that there would not be even ten righteous people in the city.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`)

## Genesis 19:15–23 (id: 12383)

* Lot escaped God's judgment because of God's grace, but Lot was still hesitant to leave Sodom. Israel would forever remember Lot as someone who hesitated and needed angels to pull him to safety. The Lord was merciful and spared Lot because of Abraham ([18:23](https://ref.ly/Gen18:23); [19:29](https://ref.ly/Gen19:29)).
* Today, people who follow God often feel pressure to copy the wrong ways that many people live. God asks his followers to live differently from people who do not follow him. This means Christians should choose actions and behaviors that show they belong to God ([1 John 2:15–17](https://ref.ly/1John2:15-1John2:17)). God will judge the corrupt systems of the world. This judgment will be even more severe than the destruction of Sodom and Gomorrah ([Matthew 11:23–24](https://ref.ly/Matt11:23-Matt11:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:15-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Zoar (ID: `place:Zoar`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`)

## Genesis 19:18–22 (id: 12384)

Lot asked the angels for permission to live in the small town of Zoar (which means "little place") even after they saved him.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:18-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zoar (ID: `place:Zoar`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`)

## Genesis 19:23–25 (id: 12386)

Compare [Luke 17:29](https://ref.ly/Luke17:29). The eruption of Mount Vesuvius, the volcano that destroyed Pompeii in AD 79, along with more recent natural disasters, shows how quickly a complete disaster like this can happen.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:23-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gomorrah (ID: `place:Gomorrah`); Sodom (ID: `place:Sodom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Zoar (ID: `place:Zoar`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`)

## Genesis 19:26 (id: 12387)

*looked back:* The verb form indicates intense gazing at the world she loved, not just a curious look ([15:5](https://ref.ly/Gen15:5); [Exodus 33:8](https://ref.ly/Exod33:8); [Numbers 21:9](https://ref.ly/Num21:9); [1 Samuel 2:32](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:32); compare [Exodus 3:6](https://ref.ly/Exod3:6)). Lot’s wife had become too attached to Sodom to follow God's calling. So God judged her as she lingered on the valley slopes. 

Christ’s return to judge the world will be as sudden and devastating as Sodom's destruction ([Luke 17:32–37](https://ref.ly/Luke17:32-Luke17:37)). Those who desire the life of this wicked world will lose both this world and the next.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:26


## Genesis 19:29 (id: 12388)

God listened to Abraham's plea (compare [18:23–32](https://ref.ly/Gen18:23-Gen18:32)). But Lot lost everything during the destruction of Sodom. His choice to live in the fertile land of Sodom eventually led him into a dangerous situation ([13:10–13](https://ref.ly/Gen13:10-Gen13:13)). After this, he could no longer remain in the land he had chosen.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 19:30–35 (id: 12389)

Sodom's culture apparently influenced Lot’s daughters more than their father's values. They had no issues having children with their drunk father because they saw no other way to continue their family line (see [9:21–22](https://ref.ly/Gen9:21-Gen9:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:30-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Zoar (ID: `place:Zoar`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Genesis 19:30–38 (id: 12390)

The poverty of the cave contrasts with the wealth Lot shared with Abram and the good life he lived in Sodom. Abraham would father a righteous nation ([17:1](https://ref.ly/Gen17:1)), but Lot and his daughters started a new Sodom.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:30-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Ammon (ID: `place:Ammon`); Ben-ammi (ID: `person:Ben-ammi`); Ammonites (ID: `group:Ammon`); Moab (ID: `person:Moab`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Zoar (ID: `place:Zoar`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Genesis 19:36–38 (id: 12391)

* The daughters' plan worked. They each became pregnant by their father. From these two sons, born from incest, came two of Israel's long\-time enemies: the nations of Moab and Ammon. Their extreme wickedness was partly due to their origins.
* Both daughters chose descriptive names for their sons. *Moab* means "from my father" and *Ben\-ammi* means "son of my people." These names subtly hinted at their actions without making people suspicious who were unaware of the stories.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 19:36-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Ammon (ID: `place:Ammon`); Ben-ammi (ID: `person:Ben-ammi`); Ammonites (ID: `group:Ammon`); Moab (ID: `person:Moab`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`)

## Genesis 20:1 (id: 12423)

Gerar was near the coast in Philistine land, about 19 kilometers (12 miles) south of Gaza and 80 kilometers (50 miles) southwest of Hebron.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shur (ID: `place:Shur`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Gerar (ID: `place:Gerar`); Kadesh-barnea (ID: `place:Kadesh-barnea`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 20:1–18 (id: 12419)

* This second "sister story" in Genesis (compare [12:10–20](https://ref.ly/Gen12:10-Gen12:20)) happened just before Sarah became pregnant with Isaac (chapter [21](https://ref.ly/Gen21:1-Gen21:34)). In both instances, God protected Abraham and Sarah's marriage to keep it pure for the covenant promises. To be part of God's plan, his people must separate from worldly corruption.
* This story happened in the promised land. It showed Israel how God intervened in people's lives to fulfill his plan. He protected them from threats from other tribes. He used his chosen people to mediate his relationship with other nations.
* By preventing adultery from destroying Abraham's marriage, God reminded the Israelites not to marry those who would lead them away from the Lord ([Ezra 9:1–4](https://ref.ly/Ezra9:1-Ezra9:4); [Nehemiah 13:23–27](https://ref.ly/Neh13:23-Neh13:27); [Malachi 2:10–17](https://ref.ly/Mal2:10-Mal2:17)). They should not allow any chance for temptation. Adultery would eventually destroy the covenant and the people.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:1-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Gerar (ID: `place:Gerar`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Innocence (ID: `keyterm:Innocence`); Shur (ID: `place:Shur`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Kadesh-barnea (ID: `place:Kadesh-barnea`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 20:2 (id: 12424)

* Abraham lied to Abimelech, just as he had lied to Pharaoh ([12:13](https://ref.ly/Gen12:13)). Isaac later did the same ([26:1–11](https://ref.ly/Gen26:1-Gen26:11)), probably learning this from his father.
* *Abimelech* (which means "my father the king") was probably a title like *Pharaoh* ([37:36](https://ref.ly/Gen37:36); [Exodus 1:15](https://ref.ly/Exod1:15)), not a personal name (see study note on Genesis 26:1).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:2
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 26:1 (ID: `12533@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gerar (ID: `place:Gerar`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`)

## Genesis 20:3 (id: 12426)

*One night … God came … in a dream:* God urgently intervened to stop Abimelech from harming Sarah's purity right before fulfilling his promise ([18:10](https://ref.ly/Gen18:10); [21:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen21:1-Gen21:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`)

## Genesis 20:3–7 (id: 12425)

God gave Abimelech a strong warning against committing adultery. This was a crime that could be punished by death (compare [Exodus 20:14](https://ref.ly/Exod20:14)). The ancient Near East considered it "tremendous guilt" ([Genesis 20:9](https://ref.ly/Gen20:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:3-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Innocence (ID: `keyterm:Innocence`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Genesis 20:4–5 (id: 12427)

* Abimelech was innocent ([20:6](https://ref.ly/Gen20:6)). He knew of no wrong he had done. But he was close to committing adultery. Not knowing the truth does not remove guilt ([Leviticus 4:13–14](https://ref.ly/Lev4:13-Lev4:14)).
* *would You destroy a nation even though it is innocent?:* Abimelech's plea to God echoes Abraham's earlier words (compare [18:23–32](https://ref.ly/Gen18:23-Gen18:32)). It shows Abraham's lack of faith at this time.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Innocence (ID: `keyterm:Innocence`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`)

## Genesis 20:6 (id: 12428)

Because Abimelech acted with a clear conscience, God kept him from sinning. God will graciously help those who try to do what is right. When people act with reverence toward God, God gives them more revelation and draws them into more specific faith (see [Acts 10](https://ref.ly/Acts10:1-Acts10:48)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Genesis 20:7 (id: 12429)

Abraham's prayer saved the king's life and restored his family ([20:17–18](https://ref.ly/Gen20:17-Gen20:18)). Abimelech learned that Abraham's God was sovereign (meaning God has complete power and authority over everything). Abraham, as God's prophet, received God's messages and would pray for others (see [Numbers 12:13](https://ref.ly/Num12:13); [Deuteronomy 9:20](https://ref.ly/Deut9:20)). This was true even if he did not always act like a prophet.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:7


## Genesis 20:8–10 (id: 12430)

Abraham received rebukes from Abimelech and God (compare [12:17–19](https://ref.ly/Gen12:17-Gen12:19)). Abimelech was upset because Abraham's lie made him guilty of this serious sin (see study note on 20:3–7). Abimelech understood that taking a married woman into his harem (a group of wives and concubines) was wrong.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:8-10
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 20:3-7 (ID: `12425@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Genesis 20:11–13 (id: 12420)

Abraham's deceit was not just a brief loss of faith. Even after being scolded in Egypt, he used this strategy everywhere he traveled (compare [12:12–13](https://ref.ly/Gen12:12-Gen12:13)). Living by faith requires perseverance.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:11-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Genesis 20:14–16 (id: 12421)

Abimelech showed he was an honorable person by making things right (see study note on 20:4–5). He made things right by letting Abraham live in the area. He also gave him slaves, livestock (compare [21:27](https://ref.ly/Gen21:27)), and 1,000 pieces of silver to make up for any wrong done to Sarah.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 20:14-16
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 20:4-5 (ID: `12427@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 20:17–18 (id: 12422)

The inability to have children suggests that some time had passed. God controls births. He decides when women can have children ([25:21](https://ref.ly/Gen25:21); [29:31](https://ref.ly/Gen29:31); [30:2](https://ref.ly/Gen30:2), [17](https://ref.ly/Gen30:17), [22–23](https://ref.ly/Gen30:22-Gen30:23); [1 Samuel 1:19–20](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:19-1Sam1:20); [Psalms 113:9](https://ref.ly/Ps113:9); [127:3](https://ref.ly/Ps127:3); [Luke 1:13](https://ref.ly/Luke1:13)).

Isaac was born 25 years after God first made the promise to Abraham (compare [12:4](https://ref.ly/Gen12:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`)

## Genesis 21:6 (id: 12445)

Sarah’s made a wordplay with the name *Isaac* (Hebrew *yitskhaq*, meaning “he laughs”). It shows how laughing in disbelief turned to laughing in joy when God fulfilled the promise ([18:12](https://ref.ly/Gen18:12)). Isaac’s name might reflect the pleasure of God and his parents at his birth. Sarah knew everyone who heard about this would laugh and celebrate with her.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`)

## Genesis 21:6–7 (id: 12444)

Sarah was filled with joy and praise for this amazing event. Only God could enable her to have a child.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 21:8–9 (id: 12447)

The feast for Isaac’s weaning probably happened when he was three. Ishmael was around 17 years old at that time ([16:16](https://ref.ly/Gen16:16)). Sarah saw Ishmael mocking her son, Isaac. The verb *metsakheq* (“mocking”) is related to the word for “laughter.” This theme ([21:6](https://ref.ly/Gen21:6)) becomes negative because of Ishmael’s mocking.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Genesis 21:8–21 (id: 12446)

God used the incident of Ishmael’s mocking Isaac to separate Ishmael and Hagar from the family and the child of promise. They would constantly threaten the promised descendant if they remained with the family.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:8-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`); Skin-Bottle (ID: `realia:Skin-bottle`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Paran (ID: `place:Paran`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Beersheba (ID: `place:Beersheba`)

## Genesis 21:10 (id: 12432)

Earlier, Sarah mistreated Hagar and forced her to flee ([16:6](https://ref.ly/Gen16:6)). Later, when Hagar’s son mistreated Isaac, Sarah insisted that the slave woman and her son leave.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`)

## Genesis 21:11–13 (id: 12433)

Abraham was upset when Sarah asked him to send Hagar and Ishmael away. God told Abraham to agree, promising that Ishmael would also have a future as Abraham's descendant.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:11-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`)

## Genesis 21:14–21 (id: 12434)

God rescued Hagar and Ishmael in the wilderness and promised them a future (compare [16:7–14](https://ref.ly/Gen16:7-Gen16:14)). This passage is like chapter [16](https://ref.ly/Gen16:1-Gen16:16), but there are key differences. God saves Hagar and Ishmael, but there is no special naming. God repeats the promise to Hagar, but she is not told to return to Sarah. 

These repeated themes show God's plan for Hagar and Ishmael. As Joseph later explained to Pharaoh, a repeated event confirms God's plan ([41:32](https://ref.ly/Gen41:32)). God did not leave Hagar and Ishmael alone. He met them in their time of despair (compare [16:7](https://ref.ly/Gen16:7)). He gave them what they needed and promised again that Ishmael would start a great nation ([21:13](https://ref.ly/Gen21:13); see [16:11–12](https://ref.ly/Gen16:11-Gen16:12)). 

Paul uses this story in his letter to the Galatians to show that God’s people must let go of anything that could block God’s promise from being fulfilled ([Galatians 4:21–31](https://ref.ly/Gal4:21-Gal4:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:14-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Skin-Bottle (ID: `realia:Skin-bottle`); Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Paran (ID: `place:Paran`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Beersheba (ID: `place:Beersheba`)

## Genesis 21:16 (id: 12435)

*a bowshot away:* This means the distance an arrow can travel when someone shoots a bow. The phrase connects with Ishmael’s future work as an archer ([21:20](https://ref.ly/Gen21:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`)

## Genesis 21:22–23 (id: 12436)

Abimelech wanted a treaty so that Abraham would not deceive him. He knew that God was blessing Abraham, even though Abraham had not always been truthful ([20:9–10](https://ref.ly/Gen20:9-Gen20:10)). This sad contradiction made the treaty necessary. 

By contrast, God’s people are called to speak truthfully. Paul teaches believers to speak the truth in love ([Ephesians 4:15](https://ref.ly/Eph4:15), [25](https://ref.ly/Eph4:25)). Jesus also warned against using oaths to distort the truth ([Matthew 5:37](https://ref.ly/Matt5:37); [James 5:12](https://ref.ly/Jas5:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:22-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Phicol (ID: `person:Phicol`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 21:22–34 (id: 12437)

At the high point of this passage, it explains the name of Beersheba, where Abraham lived ([21:31–34](https://ref.ly/Gen21:31-Gen21:34)). The name is linked to the covenant (a binding agreement) that Abraham made with the people of the land. This agreement allowed him to live there in peace and safety.

In this way, God’s promise was beginning to come true ([12:7](https://ref.ly/Gen12:7); [13:14–17](https://ref.ly/Gen13:14-Gen13:17); [15:7](https://ref.ly/Gen15:7), [18–21](https://ref.ly/Gen15:18-Gen15:21); [17:8](https://ref.ly/Gen17:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:22-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Beersheba (ID: `place:Beersheba`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Phicol (ID: `person:Phicol`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Philistines (ID: `group:Philistine`); Philistia (ID: `place:Philistine`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); Tamarisk (ID: `flora:Tamarisk`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 21:25 (id: 12438)

The theme of the well appears again (see [16:14](https://ref.ly/Gen16:14); [21:19](https://ref.ly/Gen21:19)). God provided water, symbolizing blessing, in the dry wilderness. Later, he even brought water from a rock for Israel ([Exodus 15:22–27](https://ref.ly/Exod15:22-Exod15:27); [17:1–7](https://ref.ly/Exod17:1-Exod17:7); [Numbers 20:1–13](https://ref.ly/Num20:1-Num20:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 21:27–31 (id: 12439)

Abraham gave gifts to Abimelec (compare [20:14](https://ref.ly/Gen20:14)). These gifts helped confirm his legal right to live peacefully in the land and to claim the well as his own. Beersheba marked another step toward the fulfillment (completion) of God’s promise.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:27-31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Beersheba (ID: `place:Beersheba`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 21:32 (id: 12441)

The Philistines mentioned in Genesis are not the same as the Philistines in Judges through Kings. The earlier group had Semitic (ancient Near Eastern) names, such as Abimelech, and they followed Canaanite culture.

The later Philistines were likely from the Aegean region (near Greece). They had different customs and culture. They seem to have come to Canaan by sea around 1200 BC, during the time of the judges.

Because of this, the name *Philistines* in Genesis is probably used to describe the earlier people of the region in a way that later audiences would understand.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Phicol (ID: `person:Phicol`); Philistines (ID: `group:Philistine`); Philistia (ID: `place:Philistine`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Abimelech (ID: `person:Abimelech`); Beersheba (ID: `place:Beersheba`)

## Genesis 21:33–34 (id: 12442)

* A tamarisk tree requires a lot of water. Planting it showed Abraham felt secure in his land rights and trusted God to provide water in the desert. Although he was a foreigner, living under the tree represented peaceful security (compare [Zechariah 3:10](https://ref.ly/Zech3:10)).
* *there he called upon the name of the LORD:* See study note on 12:8.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 21:33-34

## Genesis 22:1 (id: 12457)

*Some time later:* Abraham had sent Ishmael away and settled in the land. Then God tested Abraham’s faith by telling him to give up Isaac. This challenged Abraham's logic and understanding of God. 

Would he still obey when God seemed to oppose him and the covenant? Would he hold onto the boy or surrender him to God (see [Exodus 13:11–13](https://ref.ly/Exod13:11-Exod13:13))? Did he believe that God would still keep his promise and bless the world through Abraham’s descendants?

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 22:1–2 (id: 12448)

The biggest test in Abraham's life came after he finally had the promised child, Isaac, after waiting a long time. He had grown to love Isaac and enjoyed his company for many years.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mount Moriah (ID: `place:MoriahMount`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 22:2 (id: 12462)

* Abraham's earlier obedience to God's calling ([12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3)) brought him great blessings. Now, he had a chance to show even greater obedience.
* *Take your son ... Isaac, whom you love:* God described Isaac in detail to remind Abraham that Isaac was his beloved son, reinforcing the cost of the sacrifice.
* The name *Moriah* is explained by the Chronicler ([2 Chronicles 3:1](https://ref.ly/2Chr3:1)) as the future site of the temple mount in Jerusalem.
* *go:* God worded the command this way to help Abraham remember his earlier call and obey again ([12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Mount Moriah (ID: `place:MoriahMount`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`)

## Genesis 22:3 (id: 12463)

Abraham's immediate and unquestioning obedience is as remarkable as the test itself.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); To Saddle (ID: `realia:ToSaddle`)

## Genesis 22:5 (id: 12464)

*The boy and I will go over there to worship, and then we will return to you:* Abraham's amazing statement makes us wonder what he was thinking. Abraham knew that God had planned the future of the covenant around Isaac and that God wanted him to sacrifice Isaac. He could not reconcile these things in his mind; he could only do what God commanded him to do, and leave the future to God (compare [Hebrews 11:17–19](https://ref.ly/Heb11:17-Heb11:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

## Genesis 22:7–8 (id: 12465)

When Isaac asked, Abraham showed his faith in the Lord by saying, "God Himself will provide." But he did not know how. This idea is central to the entire story.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 22:9–19 (id: 12466)

God’s intervention was dramatic and instructive, confirming that he never intended for Isaac to be sacrificed. God later made it clear that child sacrifice was an abomination to him (see [Leviticus 18:21](https://ref.ly/Lev18:21); [20:1–5](https://ref.ly/Lev20:1-Lev20:5); [Deuteronomy 18:10](https://ref.ly/Deut18:10); [2 Kings 16:2–3](https://ref.ly/2Kgs16:2-2Kgs16:3); [Isaiah 57:5](https://ref.ly/Isa57:5); [Jeremiah 32:35](https://ref.ly/Jer32:35)). God wanted Abraham to sacrifice his own will and surrender it to God, and when he did, God intervened.

This story sets an example for all who worship through sacrifice. Like Abraham, true worshipers understand that everything belongs to God. They acknowledge that all things come from God and are his. A true worshiper obediently gives God what he asks for. They trust that God will provide all needs. Through this trust, they experience that God always provides.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:9-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Beersheba (ID: `place:Beersheba`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Knife (ID: `realia:Knife`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Genesis 22:11 (id: 12449)

The angel of the Lord stopped Abraham just as he was ready to plunge the knife into his son.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Genesis 22:12 (id: 12450)

* Now God knew that Abraham would hold nothing back from him, that he did truly fear God. To fear the Lord means to reverence him as sovereign, trust him implicitly, and obey him without protest. The sacrifice that pleases God is a heart broken of self\-will, surrendered to God ([Psalms 40:6–8](https://ref.ly/Ps40:6-Ps40:8); [51:17](https://ref.ly/Ps51:17)).
* *you have not withheld:* Compare [Romans 8:32](https://ref.ly/Rom8:32), which uses the same verb (“spare”) as the Greek Old Testament here. If God gave us his most precious possession, he will surely provide everything we need.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Genesis 22:13 (id: 12451)

God provided a ram caught by its horns in a bush for the sacrifice. God graciously allowed Abraham to substitute an animal sacrifice instead of Isaac. Later, all of Israel would offer animals to the Lord, understanding that God's grace allowed this substitution ([Exodus 29:10](https://ref.ly/Exod29:10); [Leviticus 4:15](https://ref.ly/Lev4:15); [16:20–22](https://ref.ly/Lev16:20-Lev16:22)). 

In the New Testament, God substituted his only son for all humanity. This perfect sacrifice was made once and for all people ([Isaiah 53:6](https://ref.ly/Isa53:6), [10](https://ref.ly/Isa53:10); [John 1:29](https://ref.ly/John1:29); [10:1–14](https://ref.ly/Heb10:1-Heb10:14); [Hebrews 7:27](https://ref.ly/Heb7:27); [1 Peter 3:18](https://ref.ly/1Pet3:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 22:14 (id: 12452)

* As in many stories about the patriarchs (early fathers of Israel), the key meaning is preserved through naming. *Yahweh\-Yireh* means "the Lord will provide" (see [22:8](https://ref.ly/Gen22:8)). The use of the divine name *Yahweh* (the personal name of God) shows that the patriarchs knew this name (compare [Exodus 6:2–3](https://ref.ly/Exod6:2-Exod6:3)), even if they did not yet understand its full meaning (see study note on Exodus 6:2–3).
* *to this day*: Later Israelites saw this story as a lesson about their worship in Jerusalem. Abraham's sacrifice happened on the mountain of the Lord, which later became the site of the temple in Jerusalem (see study note on 22:2). Three times a year, the people of Israel brought their best offerings to God for sacrifice. They trusted he would continue to provide for them.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:14
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 6:2-3 (ID: `11409@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Genesis 22:2 (ID: `12462@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`)

## Genesis 22:15–19 (id: 12453)

After the event, God again confirmed his covenant with Abraham (compare [15:5](https://ref.ly/Gen15:5), [18–21](https://ref.ly/Gen15:18-Gen15:21); [17:3–8](https://ref.ly/Gen17:3-Gen17:8)). His descendants would be numerous: 

* like the stars in the sky (compare [26:4](https://ref.ly/Gen26:4)),
* like the sand on the seashore (compare [32:12](https://ref.ly/Gen32:12)), and
* like the dust of the earth (compare [13:16](https://ref.ly/Gen13:16); [28:14](https://ref.ly/Gen28:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 22:15-19

* Sarah was 127 years old, and Isaac was 37 at this time (compare [17:17](https://ref.ly/Gen17:17)).

## Genesis 23:1–20 (id: 12467)

*She died:* Abraham bought a piece of land for Sarah's burial. This was the first sign of a permanent change, as people usually buried their dead in their ancestral homeland (compare [49:29–50:13](https://ref.ly/Gen49:29-Gen50:13)). By burying Sarah, Abraham separated himself from his ancestral home (where his relatives still lived, [22:20–24](https://ref.ly/Gen22:20-Gen22:24)). His future would be in Canaan, where his descendants would fulfill the promise.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 23:1-20

## Genesis 23:3–4 (id: 12473)

* Abraham negotiated with local Hittite elders to buy land for a burial site. These Hittites had moved south to Canaan from the large Hittite empire in what is now Turkey (compare [10:15](https://ref.ly/Gen10:15)).
* Abraham was a foreigner and an outsider among them. But he trusted God's promise that he would eventually own the land.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 23:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 23:5–6 (id: 12474)

* *sir. You are God's chosen one* (or *prince*; Hebrew *my lord*, *you are* *a* *lifted up one of God*):Either Abraham was well\-respected by these people, or they were politely asking for his generosity.
* *Bury your dead in the finest**:* They were ready to fulfill his request, especially if it meant they could legally bind him to themselves ([23:11](https://ref.ly/Gen23:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 23:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 23:7–16 (id: 12475)

Abraham wanted to buy Ephron’s cave at Machpelah, but Ephron wanted him to buy the entire field.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 23:7-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ephron (ID: `person:Ephron.2`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Shekel (ID: `realia:Shekel`); Machpelah (ID: `place:Machpelah`); Zohar (ID: `person:Zohar`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`)

## Genesis 23:9 (id: 12476)

This cave would serve as the permanent burial site for Abraham's family. It was near Mamre ([23:19](https://ref.ly/Gen23:19)), where Abraham lived (see [13:18](https://ref.ly/Gen13:18); [14:13](https://ref.ly/Gen14:13); [18:1](https://ref.ly/Gen18:1)). They would bury Abraham and Sarah, Isaac and Rebekah, and Jacob and Leah there ([23:19](https://ref.ly/Gen23:19); [25:9](https://ref.ly/Gen25:9); [35:27–29](https://ref.ly/Gen35:27-Gen35:29); [49:29–31](https://ref.ly/Gen49:29-Gen49:31); [50:13](https://ref.ly/Gen50:13)). This marked their lasting presence in the promised land.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 23:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Machpelah (ID: `place:Machpelah`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Genesis 23:11 (id: 12469)

Ephron did not intend to give the cave to Abraham for free. He expected Abraham to pay the full price. Ephron wanted to sell the cave to avoid taking care of it and to get the highest price possible.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 23:11


## Genesis 23:12–13 (id: 12470)

Abraham did not want the whole field, but he was willing to take it to get the cave.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 23:12-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ephron (ID: `person:Ephron.2`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Genesis 23:15 (id: 12471)

*four hundred shekels of silver*: This was a high price. Ephron's politeness was part of the usual bargaining process.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 23:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Shekel (ID: `realia:Shekel`)

## Genesis 23:16–20 (id: 12472)

Abraham paid the full amount and completed the deal. He avoided debt by not accepting gifts from the people (compare [14:21–24](https://ref.ly/Gen14:21-Gen14:24)). The Hittite elders witnessed the deal, ensuring no one could dispute Abraham's ownership of the land. The transaction happened at the city gate, where they conducted public legal and business matters (see [19:1](https://ref.ly/Gen19:1)). 

The land became Abraham's permanent property, a first step toward God's promise to give him the land. God had not yet fulfilled his promise with this purchase. Abraham knew it, so he planned for the future ([12:7](https://ref.ly/Gen12:7)). By buying land for his dead, he showed that God's promises continue beyond this life. This is the hope for all who die with faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 23:16-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Mamre (ID: `place:Mamre`); Machpelah (ID: `place:Machpelah`); Ephron (ID: `person:Ephron.2`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Hebron (ID: `place:Hebron`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Shekel (ID: `realia:Shekel`)

## Genesis 24:1–9 (id: 12478)

Trusting in the Lord's promise, Abraham had his main servant promise to find a wife for Isaac (probably Eliezer, [15:2](https://ref.ly/Gen15:2)). Abraham told his servant to return to his relatives in his homeland, 724 kilometers away (about 450 miles). Abraham was faithful to the covenant by planning for Isaac's future.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 24:1-9

## Genesis 24:10 (id: 12481)

Aram\-naharaim means "Aram of the two rivers." It was also known as Paddan\-aram, which means "the field of Aram" (compare [25:20](https://ref.ly/Gen25:20)). 

The journey took two weeks each way. So, the servant took 10 of his master’s camels and departed with all kinds of gifts ([24:22](https://ref.ly/Gen24:22), [53](https://ref.ly/Gen24:53)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 24:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nahor (ID: `person:Nahor.2`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`); Mesopotamia (ID: `place:Mesopotamia`)

## Genesis 24:14 (id: 12482)

Abraham’s future daughter\-in\-law showed hospitality and hard work, similar to Abraham (see [18:1–8](https://ref.ly/Gen18:1-Gen18:8)). Ten thirsty camels could drink 946 liters (250 gallons) of water. A woman willing to do that much work for a stranger was certainly not lazy, but generous and hospitable.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 24:14

## Genesis 24:27 (id: 12488)

*the LORD, the God of my master Abraham, who has not withheld His kindness and faithfulness from my master:* These words express this chapter’s message (compare [24:48](https://ref.ly/Gen24:48)). Believers can trust the Lord’s leading because he is sovereign in all things. He never leaves his people to their own resources in carrying out his covenant work.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 24:27

*Laban … rushed:* He did not want to miss the chance to marry off his sister and get more gifts. ## Genesis 24:33–48 (id: 12491)

The servant stayed focused on his mission. He insisted on sharing his story before eating. He explained his mission and recognized God's providence in leading him to Rebekah. He did this before taking care of his own needs. The servant wanted everyone to understand that this was God's work, not a chance or humanly arranged meeting.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 24:33-48

## Genesis 24:48 (id: 12493)

The way that God directed this event from behind the scenes is different than in most of Genesis, but it is true to how the life of faith normally works. Faith, expressed in personal prayer and obedience, looks for evidence of God’s working. Believers usually have to make wise choices and remain faithful to the covenant, trusting that God will guide them through the circumstances of life to accomplish his will.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 24:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Genesis 24:54–56 (id: 12496)

It was hard for Rebekah’s family to let her go so suddenly, and Laban may have hoped to gain more wealth. However, the servant had sworn an oath and would not rest until it was completed. There was no reason for him to stay.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 24:54-56

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mother of Rebekah (ID: `person:MotherOfRebekah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 24:57–58 (id: 12497)

Rebekah chose to leave at once to be with her new husband, which ended the disagreement. She accepted what seemed to be the Lord’s clear leading.

In that culture, young women usually wanted to marry (not marrying brought great shame and hardship). Later stories about Laban help explain why Rebekah may have wanted to leave (compare [31:14–15](https://ref.ly/Gen31:14-Gen31:15)). 

Rebekah showed faithful love to the servant, to Abraham’s family, and to Isaac by going to become his wife.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 24:57-58

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rebekah (ID: `person:Rebekah`)

## Genesis 24:60 (id: 12499)

When Rebekah left, her family blessed her, hoping she would become the mother of millions. They said, *May your* *offspring possess the gates of their enemies*. Rebekah's marriage to Isaac was part of God's plan to bless "all the families of the earth" ([12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 24:60

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Rebekah (ID: `person:Rebekah`)

## Genesis 25:5–6 (id: 12526)

Abraham loved all his sons, so before he died, he gave them gifts and sent them away as he had sent Ishmael ([21:8–14](https://ref.ly/Gen21:8-Gen21:14)). This made certain that Isaac remained his heir.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 25:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`)

## Genesis 25:7–8 (id: 12527)

These verses mention Abraham's death before Jacob and Esau are born. But he actually lived until they were 15 years old ([25:19–26](https://ref.ly/Gen25:19-Gen25:26); compare [21:5](https://ref.ly/Gen21:5); [25:26](https://ref.ly/Gen25:26)). This storytelling choice ends Abraham's story before focusing on Isaac's family.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 25:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 25:11 (id: 12503)

*God blessed his son Isaac.* Abraham had sent his other sons away. Isaac lived near Beer\-lahai\-roi, a special place where God answered prayers ([16:14](https://ref.ly/Gen16:14)) and where Isaac waited for the Lord ([24:62](https://ref.ly/Gen24:62)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 25:11

## Genesis 25:12–18 (id: 12504)

This record lists Ishmael’s descendants before giving the details of Isaac’s descendants (see [25:19–35:29](https://ref.ly/Gen25:19-Gen35:29)). This follows the literary structure of Genesis (see Genesis Book Introduction, “Summary”).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 25:12-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Mishma (ID: `person:Mishma`); Jetur (ID: `person:Jetur`); Tema (ID: `person:Tema`); Mibsam (ID: `person:Mibsam`); Kedemah (ID: `person:Kedemah`); Dumah (ID: `person:Dumah`); Massa (ID: `person:Massa`); Naphish (ID: `person:Naphish`); Havilah (ID: `place:Havilah.2`); Nebaioth (ID: `person:Nebaioth`); Hadad (ID: `person:Hadad.4`); Adbeel (ID: `person:Adbeel`); Kedar (ID: `person:Kedar`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Shur (ID: `place:Shur`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Hagar (ID: `person:Hagar`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 25:16 (id: 12505)

Ishmael's 12 sons fulfilled God's promise of blessings ([Genesis 17:20](https://ref.ly/Gen17:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 25:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`)

## Genesis 25:18 (id: 12506)

* Havilah was a region in north\-central Arabia.
* Shur was an area between Beersheba and Egypt.
* *And they lived in hostility toward all their brothers:* This wording is similar to [16:12](https://ref.ly/Gen16:12).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 25:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Havilah (ID: `place:Havilah.2`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Shur (ID: `place:Shur`)

## Genesis 25:19–20 (id: 12507)

*Isaac … married Rebekah:* Isaac’s marriage connected him and his family more closely to Abraham’s relatives. If he had married a Canaanite woman, the faith of the covenant could have been endangered by these corrupt people and their mixed worship.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 25:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Paddan-aram (ID: `place:Paddan-aram`); Arameans (ID: `group:Aramean`); Laban (ID: `person:Laban`); Rebekah (ID: `person:Rebekah`); Bethuel (ID: `person:Bethuel`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 25:19–26 (id: 12508)

Jacob’s struggle to be greater than his brother began before the twins were born (see [Hosea 12:3](https://ref.ly/Hos12:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 25:19-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Esau (ID: `person:Esau`); Rebekah (ID: `person:Rebekah`); Paddan-aram (ID: `place:Paddan-aram`); Arameans (ID: `group:Aramean`); Laban (ID: `person:Laban`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Bethuel (ID: `person:Bethuel`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)

This account of the family of Isaac, the chosen son of Abraham, mostly recounts Jacob’s struggle for the blessing ([25:27–34](https://ref.ly/Gen25:27-Gen25:34); chapters [27–33](https://ref.ly/Gen27:1-Gen33:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 25:19
