## Genesis 35:1–29 (id: 12690)

* This chapter discusses God's promises, Jacob's vow, and how Jacob's sons continue the covenant. Deborah, Rachel, and Isaac died. This marked the end of an era and the story of Isaac's family ([25:19–35:29](https://ref.ly/Gen25:19-Gen35:29)).
* They removed idols ([35:1–4](https://ref.ly/Gen35:1-Gen35:4)) and established pure worship ([35:6–7](https://ref.ly/Gen35:6-Gen35:7)). During this time, they needed to renew their faith so Jacob's sons could continue the covenant.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 35:1-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bethel (ID: `place:Bethel`); Rachel (ID: `person:Rachel`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Bethlehem (ID: `place:Bethlehem`); Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Bilhah (ID: `person:Bilhah`); Esau (ID: `person:Esau`); Paddan-aram (ID: `place:Paddan-aram`); Leah (ID: `person:Leah`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Allon-bachuth (ID: `place:Allon-bacuth`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); A Midwife (ID: `person:GenericMidwife`); Deborah (ID: `person:Deborah`); Tower of Eder (ID: `place:TowerOfEder`); Mamre (ID: `place:Mamre`); Issachar (ID: `person:Issachar`); Hebron (ID: `place:Hebron`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Zilpah (ID: `person:Zilpah`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Gad (ID: `person:Gad`); Zebulun (ID: `person:Zebulun`); Naphtali (ID: `person:Naphtali`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`); Dan (ID: `person:Dan`); Rebekah (ID: `person:Rebekah`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Shechem (ID: `place:Shechem`); Asher (ID: `person:Asher`); Terebinth (ID: `flora:Terebinth`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Oak (ID: `flora:Oak`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Earring (ID: `realia:Earring`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Genesis 35:2–4 (id: 12697)

Jacob vowed to fully devote himself to the Lord ([28:20–22](https://ref.ly/Gen28:20-Gen28:22)). To establish this devotion, his family had to get rid of all pagan idols and stop worshiping other gods. God allows no rivals. Only the Lord could be their God (compare [Joshua 5:1–9](https://ref.ly/Josh5:1-Josh5:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 35:2-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Terebinth (ID: `flora:Terebinth`); Pure (ID: `keyterm:Pure`); Shechem (ID: `place:Shechem`); Bethel (ID: `place:Bethel`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Earring (ID: `realia:Earring`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

*He has been with me wherever I have gone:* God had fulfilled his promises ([28:15](https://ref.ly/Gen28:15); [31:3](https://ref.ly/Gen31:3)), so Jacob must fulfill his vow.

## Genesis 35:11–12 (id: 12692)

God’s reference to himself as *God Almighty* assured Jacob that his promise could and would be fulfilled. At Bethel, God promised Jacob that he would have descendants in the land (compare [28:13–14](https://ref.ly/Gen28:13-Gen28:14)). Here, God added that his descendants would include kings (see [17:6](https://ref.ly/Gen17:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 35:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`)

## Genesis 35:14–15 (id: 12693)

In fulfilling his vow ([28:20–22](https://ref.ly/Gen28:20-Gen28:22)), Jacob’s actions were almost identical to his actions in the earlier experience at Bethel (compare [28:16–19](https://ref.ly/Gen28:16-Gen28:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 35:14-15

## Genesis 35:16–20 (id: 12694)

Benjamin's birth completed the family. But it was a sad event because Rachel died during childbirth (see study note on 31:32).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 35:16-20
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 31:32 (ID: `12641@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rachel (ID: `person:Rachel`); Bethlehem (ID: `place:Bethlehem`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); A Midwife (ID: `person:GenericMidwife`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Bethel (ID: `place:Bethel`)

## Genesis 35:18 (id: 12695)

Rachel felt that the name Ben\-oni, meaning “son of my sorrow,” fit the moment. But Jacob did not want his son to carry such a sad name, so he changed it to Benjamin, meaning “son of my right hand.” In this way, Jacob changed a moment of sorrow into one of hope and gave his son a name that pointed to blessing and strength.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 35:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 35:19 (id: 12696)

Jacob did not carry Rachel’s body to the family tomb at Machpelah ([23:1–20](https://ref.ly/Gen23:1-Gen23:20); [25:9](https://ref.ly/Gen25:9); [49:30](https://ref.ly/Gen49:30); [50:13](https://ref.ly/Gen50:13)) but buried her in the territory that would be Benjamin’s (see [Josh 18:21–28](https://ref.ly/Josh18:21-Josh18:28); [1 Sam 10:2](https://ref.ly/1Sam10:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 35:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethlehem (ID: `place:Bethlehem`); Rachel (ID: `person:Rachel`)

## Genesis 35:20 (id: 12698)

*it marks Rachel’s tomb to this day**:* This seems to be a later editorial comment by someone who was living in the land after the conquest and giving directions to the tomb (see Genesis Book Introduction, “Composition”).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 35:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Rachel (ID: `person:Rachel`)

## Genesis 35:22 (id: 12699)

* Reuben had sexual relations with Bilhah. This dishonored his father's marriage. As the oldest son, Reuben might have been trying to take over as the clan leader using a pagan method (compare [2 Samuel 16:15–22](https://ref.ly/2Sam16:15-2Sam16:22)). But this act caused him to lose his birthright (see [Genesis 49:3–4](https://ref.ly/Gen49:3-Gen49:4)).
* Jacob soon found out what happened, but he delayed his response again (see [49:3–4](https://ref.ly/Gen49:3-Gen49:4); compare [34:5](https://ref.ly/Gen34:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 35:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); Bilhah (ID: `person:Bilhah`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

The 12 sons of Jacob became the leaders of the 12 tribes of Israel. Their names are the beginnings of the nation.

## Genesis 36:1–8 (id: 12707)

The story of Esau emphasizes two main points: 

1. The *sons of Esau … were born to him in the land of Canaan* ([36:5](https://ref.ly/Gen36:5)) before he moved to Seir ([36:8](https://ref.ly/Gen36:8)). In contrast, most of Jacob’s children were born outside Canaan but later moved there. God gave the land to Jacob and his descendants, making space for them by settling Esau elsewhere.
2. Esau was also known as Edom. Israel often had conflicts with the Edomites (see [1 Samuel 21:7](https://ref.ly/1Sam21:7); [22:9–22](https://ref.ly/1Sam22:9-1Sam22:22); [Obadiah 1](https://ref.ly/Obad1:1-Obad1:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 36:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Esau (ID: `person:Esau`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Oholibamah (ID: `person:Oholibamah`); Adah (ID: `person:Adah.2`); Mahalath (ID: `person:Mahalath`); Eliphaz (ID: `person:Eliphaz`); Anah (ID: `person:Anah`); Jalam (ID: `person:Jalam`); Elon (ID: `person:Elon`); Jeush (ID: `person:Jeush`); Reuel (ID: `person:Reuel.3`); Zibeon (ID: `person:Zibeon`); Korah (ID: `person:Korah`); Seir (ID: `place:Seir`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Nebaioth (ID: `person:Nebaioth`); Hivites (ID: `group:Hivites`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 36:2–3 (id: 12709)

Esau had three wives named *Adah*, *Oholibamah*, and *Basemath*. Two of these names differ from earlier mentions ([26:34](https://ref.ly/Gen26:34); [28:9](https://ref.ly/Gen28:9)). It is possible the other wives died, Esau preferred these three out of six, or these were different names for the same wives. There is not enough information to know for sure.

## Genesis 37:1 (id: 12723)

In chapter [36](https://ref.ly/Gen36:1-Gen36:43), Esau was increasing in power and wealth. In contrast, Jacob, still waiting for the promise, lived in the land as a foreigner, like his father. He remained a temporary resident with a single family. 

Worldly greatness often comes more quickly than spiritual greatness. Waiting for the promised spiritual blessing while others prosper tests patience, faith, and perseverance.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 37:2 (id: 12731)

* The account of Jacob and his family now turns to Joseph and his brothers. Jacob is still important, but the story focuses on Joseph, who is introduced as an obedient seventeen\-year\-old son.
* *he brought their father a bad report about them:* Bringing a bad report is not popular, but it was the right thing to do. It shows that Joseph was faithful from the beginning. As the story continues, his brothers’ wicked behavior becomes clearer. In the end, like Cain, they even tried to kill the brother who pleased God.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bilhah (ID: `person:Bilhah`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Zilpah (ID: `person:Zilpah`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 37:2–50.26 (id: 12724)

* The story of Joseph and his brothers is a distinct section in Genesis. It has a different tone and focus from the earlier parts. The story follows one connected series of events, with Joseph at the center.
* Repeated themes structure the entire story of Joseph. These themes are similar to those in wisdom books like Proverbs, Ecclesiastes, and Daniel.

    Similar events that happen more than once shape the whole story. Its themes are like those in wisdom books (books that teach how to live well), such as Proverbs, Ecclesiastes, and Daniel. These books teach that God can bring good out of evil and joy out of suffering.

    Even if this does not fully happen in this life, it will happen in the life to come. Although the wicked may succeed for a time, the righteous (those who live rightly before God) should remain faithful. A deeper and lasting way of life comes through obedience to God.

    Anyone who hopes to be a leader in God’s plan can learn from Joseph’s story. Wisdom guided him and led to his success. Jesus Christ perfectly lived out this wisdom because he is the wisdom of God.

* Judah is also an important person in the story. At first, he was careless and unkind, like his brothers. But later, he truly repented (turned away from his sin). He was willing to give his life to save a younger brother for his father. Because of this, he received an important inheritance.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Shelah (ID: `person:Shelah`); Hebrews (ID: `group:Hebrews`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Nile River (ID: `place:Nile`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`)

## Genesis 37:3 (id: 12737)

* *Israel* (or *Jacob*): See also [37:13](https://ref.ly/Gen37:13).
* Israel loved Joseph more than his other children. Joseph was born when Israel was old. He was the first son of Israel's favorite wife, Rachel.
* Israel gave Joseph a beautiful robe to show he planned to give him the largest share of the inheritance.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 37:4 (id: 12738)

Jacob’s favoritism (treating one child better than the others) toward Joseph made his other sons hate him even more. In the same way, Isaac’s and Rebekah’s favoritism had divided their family. Now Jacob’s favoritism would lead to separation between him and his son Joseph.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`)

## Genesis 37:5–11 (id: 12739)

God confirmed his choice of this faithful son as the future leader of the family through two symbolic dreams. This is the first of three dream sequences in chapters [37–50](https://ref.ly/Gen37:1-Gen50:26) (see [40:1–41:36](https://ref.ly/Gen40:1-Gen41:36); compare [15:13](https://ref.ly/Gen15:13); [20:3](https://ref.ly/Gen20:3); [28:12–15](https://ref.ly/Gen28:12-Gen28:15); [31:24](https://ref.ly/Gen31:24)). Dreams were often seen as a way God spoke to people. People considered a dream to be more important if it was given twice (repeated to show it was certain).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:5-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Sheaf (ID: `realia:Sheaf`)

## Genesis 37:7 (id: 12740)

The *sheaves of grain* suggest how Joseph would gain authority over his family (see [42:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen42:1-Gen42:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sheaf (ID: `realia:Sheaf`)

## Genesis 37:8 (id: 12741)

The brothers reacted angrily to the revelation, unlike Joseph, who was honest and faithful. This shows why they were not chosen as leaders. Leaders in God's plan cannot be full of jealousy and hatred. 

In their anger, they missed an important part of the revelation. They also would become rulers ("stars," [37:9](https://ref.ly/Gen37:9)) and would successfully gather their sheaves ([37:7](https://ref.ly/Gen37:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:8


## Genesis 37:9 (id: 12742)

*the sun and moon and eleven stars:* Signs like the sun, moon, and stars often stand for rulers. The dream showed that Joseph would become a leader over the whole family of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:9


## Genesis 37:10 (id: 12718)

*your mother … and I:* Joseph's birth mother, Rachel, was dead ([35:19](https://ref.ly/Gen35:19)). Leah was now the matriarch of the clan.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:10


## Genesis 37:11 (id: 12719)

* Joseph’s brothers hated him because they were jealous. Instead of recognizing that God was at work, they tried to stop his dreams from coming true ([37:18–36](https://ref.ly/Gen37:18-Gen37:36)). These actions show that they were not ready to lead God’s people.

    God’s choice of a leader (especially someone young or who seems unqualified) often reveals what is in people’s hearts. It shows whether they are willing to accept that leader’s authority. Joseph’s brothers represent many people throughout history who act with envy (jealous anger) and malice (a desire to harm others) because they are not committed to doing the Lord’s will.

* Jacob wondered what the dreams meant because he knew God would choose the next leader. He understood that God could choose the younger son to lead the older ones and could reveal all this in dreams.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`)

## Genesis 37:12–13 (id: 12720)

It was foolish for Jacob to send Joseph on such a mission, knowing how the brothers felt about him. Joseph obeyed his father by going to find his brothers despite their hatred for him.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:12-13

## Genesis 37:25–28 (id: 12727)

The Ishmaelite traders were descendants of the slave child who was cast out for mocking Isaac. Now they would enslave Joseph. When he was sold for 20 pieces of silver and carried to Egypt, he was at least preserved alive.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:25-28

## Genesis 37:26 (id: 12728)

Judah began to take a leadership role, which he continued to develop as the story goes on (see [43:8–10](https://ref.ly/Gen43:8-Gen43:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Genesis 37:28 (id: 12729)

* The Ishmaelites are called Midianite traders (that is, traders from Midian). Ishmaelites were descendants of Abraham through Hagar ([16:5](https://ref.ly/Gen16:5)). Midianites were descendants of Abraham through Keturah ([25:1–2](https://ref.ly/Gen25:1-Gen25:2)). The term *Ishmaelite* may have been used more generally for desert tribes (nomadic groups who move from place to place). The Midianites may also have been traveling with a separate group of Ishmaelite traders ([37:27](https://ref.ly/Gen37:27)).
* Kidnapping (taking a person by force) is mentioned in [40:15](https://ref.ly/Gen40:15). Later, it became a capital offense (a crime punished by death) in Israel’s law (see [Exodus 21:16](https://ref.ly/Exod21:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 37:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Midianites (ID: `group:Midian`); Ishmaelites (ID: `group:Ishmael`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Pit (ID: `realia:Pit`)

## Genesis 38:1–30 (id: 12743)

The story of Judah and Tamar is a carefully placed break in the larger story. It shows what was happening in Judah’s family. Judah would later become an important leader. The story also shows the early stages of the family mixing with the people of the land. This helps explain why God sent the family to Egypt (chapters [39–47](https://ref.ly/Gen39:1-Gen47:31)). 

The Egyptians kept themselves separate from other peoples ([43:32](https://ref.ly/Gen43:32)). Because of that, the Israelites were more likely to keep their own identity in Egypt than in Canaan.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 38:1-30

## Genesis 38:8 (id: 12756)

*Perform your duty* *...* *raise up offspring for your brother:* This reflects the custom of levirate marriage (a practice where a man marries his dead brother’s wife).

In this custom, if a man died without children, his brother or nearest relative would marry the widow. The goal was to have a child who would carry on the dead man’s name and inherit his property. This practice was later included in God’s law for Israel ([Deuteronomy 25:5–10](https://ref.ly/Deut25:5-Deut25:10)). 

A close relative could refuse this duty, but he would be publicly shamed in the family for not preserving his brother’s name.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 38:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Onan (ID: `person:Onan`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Genesis 38:9–10 (id: 12757)

Onan married Tamar, but he was not willing to provide an heir for his brother. He had sexual relations with Tamar, but he did not fulfill his duty to his dead brother ([38:8](https://ref.ly/Gen38:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 38:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Onan (ID: `person:Onan`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 38:11 (id: 12744)

Judah is now shown as the example for his sons' behavior. He also failed in his duty under levirate marriage toward Tamar, even though he was the next kinsman (see study note on 38:8). Judah and his sons acted too much like the Canaanites (see study note on 38:27–30; this contrasts with Boaz, who acted rightly in [Ruth 3–4](https://ref.ly/Ruth3:1-Ruth4:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 38:11

## Genesis 38:14–19 (id: 12746)

Tamar understood she needed to act to secure her family's future. Following the levirate custom (see study note on 38:8), she remained loyal to her dead husband. She had a legal right to have a child with Judah's son or Judah himself. So she tricked her father\-in\-law into having sexual relations with her. Again, Jacob’s family experiences deception, this time by a Canaanite daughter\-in\-law.

## Genesis 38:26 (id: 12751)

*She is more righteous than I:* Judah admitted he had failed to provide an heir. It was wrong for Judah to visit a prostitute, but Tamar had a legal right to have Judah's child and acted on it. 

In the book of Ruth, the elders gave a similar blessing at the marriage of Boaz and Ruth. They prayed that God would make Ruth like Tamar ([Ruth 4:12](https://ref.ly/Ruth4:12); compare [Matthew 1:3](https://ref.ly/Matt1:3), [5](https://ref.ly/Matt1:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 38:26

## Genesis 38:27–30 (id: 12752)

Judah’s family line continued through Tamar. The twins replaced Judah’s two sons who had died ([38:7](https://ref.ly/Gen38:7), [10](https://ref.ly/Gen38:10)). Their birth was similar to the birth of Jacob and Esau ([25:21–26](https://ref.ly/Gen25:21-Gen25:26)). As with Jacob and Esau, the expected order of birth was reversed. In both cases, the twin connected with the color red (*scarlet*) did not remain in the first place.

In a similar way, Jacob gained the right to rule over his older brother ([27:29](https://ref.ly/Gen27:29)). This pattern seems to appear again in Judah’s family. The family line continued through Perez, not through the older son Shelah, whom Judah had tried hard to protect ([38:11](https://ref.ly/Gen38:11); see [1 Chronicles 4:21](https://ref.ly/1Chr4:21)), and not through the older twin Zerah (see [Ruth 4:13–22](https://ref.ly/Ruth4:13-Ruth4:22); [Matthew 1:3](https://ref.ly/Matt1:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 38:27-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perez (ID: `person:Perez`); Zerah (ID: `person:Zerah.3`)

## Genesis 39:6–10 (id: 12765)

A major theme in wisdom literature (wise teaching about how to live well; see study note on 37:2–50:26) is a warning to young people: sexual sin and other wrong behavior will lead to harm and ruin (compare [Proverbs 5–7](https://ref.ly/Prov5:1-Prov7:24)). Joseph resisted temptation because he had godly wisdom. He was guided by the fear of the Lord (a deep respect and reverence for God that leads to obedience; [Proverbs 1:7](https://ref.ly/Prov1:7); [9:10](https://ref.ly/Prov9:10)).

Joseph did not lose faith in God’s promises, as evidenced by his readiness to interpret the dreams of two prisoners. ## Genesis 41:1–4 (id: 12795)

## Genesis 41:1–46 (id: 12794)

God had used two dreams to identify Joseph as a leader among his brothers ([37:5–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:5-Gen37:11)). He used two dreams to test Joseph’s faith in prison ([40:5–14](https://ref.ly/Gen40:5-Gen40:14)). Now God used two more dreams to raise Joseph from prison to a place of high honor and authority.

Joseph had shown that he was faithful in small tasks. Now God would give him responsibility over greater things.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:1-46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Nile River (ID: `place:Nile`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Potiphera (ID: `person:Potiphera`); Asenath (ID: `person:Asenath`); On (ID: `place:On`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Hebrews (ID: `group:Hebrews`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Genesis 41:5–7 (id: 12809)

Pharaoh's second dream had a similar message. Seven shriveled heads of grain ate seven full heads of grain on one stalk and withered heads that grew afterward.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`)

## Genesis 41:8 (id: 12812)

The magicians and wise men were part of a group of people who claimed special skill in spiritual matters, such as understanding dreams and visions (see [Exodus 8:18–19](https://ref.ly/Exod8:18-Exod8:19); [Daniel 2:10–11](https://ref.ly/Dan2:10-Dan2:11)). 

God used an Israelite slave to show that the wisdom of this world is limited (see [Daniel 2](https://ref.ly/Dan2:1-Dan2:49)). Even the most powerful nation is still under God’s rule and control ([Daniel 2:20–23](https://ref.ly/Dan2:20-Dan2:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`)

## Genesis 41:9–13 (id: 12813)

The chief cup\-bearer finally remembered Joseph and testified that his interpretations were true.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:9-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Hebrews (ID: `group:Hebrews`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 41:14–15 (id: 12796)

* Pharaoh quickly summoned Joseph from prison to explain his dreams.
* Joseph shaved, following the Egyptian custom.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Genesis 41:16 (id: 12797)

Joseph knew that only God could explain Pharaoh’s dreams (compare[40:8](https://ref.ly/Gen40:8)). He was sure God would reveal their meaning, as the dreams had a purpose ([41:25](https://ref.ly/Gen41:25), [28](https://ref.ly/Gen41:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`)

## Genesis 41:17–24 (id: 12798)

Pharaoh recounted his dreams and testified that no human wisdom could interpret them.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:17-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Nile River (ID: `place:Nile`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 41:25–32 (id: 12799)

Both dreams predicted that seven years of abundant crops would be followed by seven years of severe famine.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:25-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 41:32 (id: 12800)

The two similar dreams showed that*the matter has been decreed by God, and He will carry it out shortly.* This was like the dreams of the two prisoners that God quickly fulfilled ([40:5–23](https://ref.ly/Gen40:5-Gen40:23)). Joseph’s own two dreams ([37:5–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:5-Gen37:11)) were also about to come true ([41:37–46](https://ref.ly/Gen41:37-Gen41:46); [42:6–9](https://ref.ly/Gen42:6-Gen42:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`)

## Genesis 41:33–36 (id: 12801)

* God’s message required a response. It was not given just to satisfy curiosity about the future. Joseph’s advice showed that he was wise and able to plan well. This is why Pharaoh chose him to lead ([41:37–40](https://ref.ly/Gen41:37-Gen41:40)).
* Joseph set up a system to store food. He placed one main leader over the work and local leaders in each area. He collected one\-fifth (20 percent) of the grain and stored it for future needs. Later, wisdom literature (see study note on 37:2–50:26) teaches the same idea: plan for the future instead of living only for the present (see [Proverbs 6:6–8](https://ref.ly/Prov6:6-Prov6:8); [27:12](https://ref.ly/Prov27:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:33-36
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 37:2-50.26 (ID: `12724@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`)

## Genesis 41:37–40 (id: 12802)

Pharaoh saw that Joseph was the right person for this work. He recognized that Joseph had the Spirit of God and was wise and understanding.

In this way, God showed his rule and authority over Egypt. Israelites who later read this story could trust that God would save them, just as he had promised.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:37-40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 41:41–46 (id: 12803)

Joseph was made the acting ruler or manager of Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:41-46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Potiphera (ID: `person:Potiphera`); Asenath (ID: `person:Asenath`); On (ID: `place:On`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`)

## Genesis 41:42 (id: 12804)

* Pharaoh’s signet ring had a seal used for signing documents. The seal was impressed in soft clay, which hardened and left a permanent impression of the ruler’s signature, which carried his authority. Numerous seals of this type have been found in archaeological digs.
* The linen clothing and gold chain showed Joseph's new status as a ruler.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Genesis 41:43–44 (id: 12805)

Pharaoh made Joseph second\-in\-command; all the people had to submit to him. Compare [Psalm 105:16–22](https://ref.ly/Ps105:16-Ps105:22).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:43-44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`)

## Genesis 41:45 (id: 12806)

* As a sign of Joseph's new status, Pharaoh gave him an Egyptian name and a wife from an important family.
* On was a center for sun worship and later came to be known as Heliopolis (meaning "sun city").

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Potiphera (ID: `person:Potiphera`); Asenath (ID: `person:Asenath`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); On (ID: `place:On`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Genesis 41:46 (id: 12807)

* *Joseph was thirty years old:* It had been about 13 years since his brothers sold Joseph into slavery ([37:2](https://ref.ly/Gen37:2)).
* *traveled throughout the land of Egypt:* As a wise manager, his first priority was to understand his responsibilities.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 41:47–57 (id: 12808)

Pharaoh's dreams were fulfilled just as Joseph had explained.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:47-57

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Potiphera (ID: `person:Potiphera`); Asenath (ID: `person:Asenath`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); On (ID: `place:On`)

## Genesis 41:50–52 (id: 12810)

* Despite his status and power, Joseph stayed true to his heritage. He gave his two sons Hebrew names.
* *Manasseh:* Joseph's wealth and success helped him *forget* the pain of being away from his family.
* *Ephraim:* By giving him this name, Joseph expressed his thanks to God for making him *fruitful* in Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:50-52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Potiphera (ID: `person:Potiphera`); Asenath (ID: `person:Asenath`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); On (ID: `place:On`)

## Genesis 41:53–57 (id: 12811)

Joseph’s wisdom paid off, for the seven years of plenty were followed by seven years of severe famine, but throughout Egypt there was plenty of food. Joseph had grain to sell to the Egyptians and to people from other countries as well.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:53-57

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`)

## Genesis 42:1–44.34 (id: 12814)

Joseph did several unusual things to his brothers when they came looking for grain. The last time Joseph had been with them, they were filled with jealousy, hatred, and anger; they attempted to destroy their brother, and they deceived their father. Joseph put them through various tests, similar to the trying situations they had put him through, to see if they had changed.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:1-44:34

God used the famine to bring Israel to Egypt under Joseph’s rule, thus fulfilling two prophecies ([15:13](https://ref.ly/Gen15:13); [37:7–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:7-Gen37:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:1-2

## Genesis 42:4 (id: 12829)

Jacob may have believed that Benjamin would not be safe with his brothers.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 42:6–7 (id: 12830)

Joseph's first dream ([37:5–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:5-Gen37:11)) was partially fulfilled when his brothers bowed down to him without recognizing him (see also [43:26](https://ref.ly/Gen43:26); [44:14](https://ref.ly/Gen44:14)). It was totally fulfilled in [50:18](https://ref.ly/Gen50:18). Joseph recognized his brothers immediately, but he could not reveal himself because he did not yet trust them to be the honest men they claimed to be ([42:10](https://ref.ly/Gen42:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`)

## Genesis 42:8 (id: 12831)

*they did not recognize him:* This was because Joseph was now a grown man, not a boy. He did not have a beard, wore Egyptian clothes, and was in an unexpected position of power. He spoke to them through an interpreter ([42:23](https://ref.ly/Gen42:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 42:9 (id: 12832)

*You are spies!:* The brothers thought Joseph was a spy for their father and treated him harshly ([37:2](https://ref.ly/Gen37:2), [14](https://ref.ly/Gen37:14), [18–28](https://ref.ly/Gen37:18-Gen37:28)). Now, Joseph was testing them in a similar way to see their reaction.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 42:11 (id: 12816)

Joseph knew that they had not always been the honest men they claimed to be.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:11


## Genesis 42:15–17 (id: 12817)

Joseph put his brothers in prison for three days. He did this to see if they felt guilt for what they had done in the past ([42:21–23](https://ref.ly/Gen42:21-Gen42:23)). The brothers had similarly thrown Joseph into a cistern\-prison while they decided what to do with him ([37:24](https://ref.ly/Gen37:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:15-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Genesis 42:18–20 (id: 12818)

Rather than keep all but one, Joseph would release all but one to take grain home to their starving families.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:18-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 42:21–23 (id: 12819)

*Surely we are being punished:* The brothers believed that God was punishing them. They thought this because they now had to bring Benjamin to Egypt, even though their father did not want this. They connected this trouble with their past sin of selling Joseph to traders.

They began to see this as divine punishment (God judging wrong actions). The fear of divine punishment began to cause feelings of guilt that Joseph's cries for mercy and their father's tears had failed to produce ([37:34–35](https://ref.ly/Gen37:34-Gen37:35)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:21-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); To Beg for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:BegForMercy`)

## Genesis 42:22 (id: 12820)

*you would not listen:* Reuben had lost his place as leader (see study notes on 42:37; 49:3–4).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:22
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 42:37 (ID: `12827@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Genesis 49:3-4 (ID: `12914@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Genesis 42:24 (id: 12821)

Joseph turned away from them and began to weep (compare [43:30](https://ref.ly/Gen43:30); [45:2](https://ref.ly/Gen45:2), [14](https://ref.ly/Gen45:14); [50:1](https://ref.ly/Gen50:1), [17](https://ref.ly/Gen50:17)). He might have heard a part of the story he had never known, like Reuben’s attempt to save him ([37:21–22](https://ref.ly/Gen37:21-Gen37:22), [29](https://ref.ly/Gen37:29)). Or, his brothers’ remorse might have moved him to forgive them.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`)

## Genesis 42:25 (id: 12823)

*return each man’s silver to his sack:* Joseph was testing his brothers. He wanted to appeal to their conscience (their inner sense of right and wrong) and make them face their past guilt. Once again, they were going home with silver instead of a brother ([37:28–35](https://ref.ly/Gen37:28-Gen37:35)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`)

## Genesis 42:25–28 (id: 12822)

Joseph cared for his brothers' needs. He forgave them and provided for them (see study note on 42:24). God used Joseph's care to make the brothers more aware of their sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:25-28
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 42:24 (ID: `12821@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 42:28 (id: 12824)

*What is this that God has done to us?:* They realized God was responsible for everything happening, so they faced a day of reckoning for their sins.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`)

## Genesis 42:29–34 (id: 12825)

The brothers’ report focused on the charge that they were spies and on the need to bring Benjamin back to Egypt with them. They did not mention that they were beginning to see God’s judgment (his just response to wrongdoing) for what they had done to Joseph.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:29-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 42:36 (id: 12826)

* Jacob was already grieving the loss of two sons and feared losing Benjamin if he went to Egypt.
* *You have deprived me of my sons:* He did not know how true his words were. But these words probably troubled his sons' guilty consciences.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 42:37 (id: 12827)

Reuben tried to take charge. He might have thought he could regain his father's favor (see [35:22](https://ref.ly/Gen35:22)). First, he hoped to rescue Joseph from death ([37:21–22](https://ref.ly/Gen37:21-Gen37:22), [29–30](https://ref.ly/Gen37:29-Gen37:30)), and now by keeping Benjamin safe.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`)

## Genesis 42:38 (id: 12828)

Jacob continued to show special favor to Rachel’s only remaining son. He refused to let Benjamin go to Egypt, even if it meant that Simeon, Leah’s son, would not return.

Jacob’s deep grief later weighed heavily on the brothers’ conscience ([44:18–34](https://ref.ly/Gen44:18-Gen44:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Genesis 43:1–7 (id: 12833)

As the famine continued, Jacob's family needed more grain. But, they could not go back to Egypt without Benjamin ([42:16](https://ref.ly/Gen42:16), [20](https://ref.ly/Gen42:20)). Jacob understood he was in a difficult situation. He needed more food but he would not risk losing Benjamin.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 43:8–10 (id: 12845)

Judah solved the problem by taking responsibility for Benjamin’s safety. In this way, he succeeded where Reuben had failed. This change was important because Judah had earlier suggested selling Joseph to the Ishmaelites ([37:26–27](https://ref.ly/Gen37:26-Gen37:27)) instead of killing him. Now he would secure Benjamin’s safety with his own life (see [44:18–34](https://ref.ly/Gen44:18-Gen44:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:8-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 43:11–13 (id: 12834)

Jacob provided everything he could to ensure a favorable reception from the Egyptian governor ("the man"; compare [32:13–21](https://ref.ly/Gen32:13-Gen32:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:11-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gum Tragacanth (ID: `flora:GumTragacanth`); Almond (ID: `flora:Almond`); Pistachio Nuts (ID: `flora:PistachioNuts`); Rock Rose (ID: `flora:Ladanum`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Terebinth (ID: `flora:Terebinth`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Liquidambar (ID: `flora:Liquidambar`)

## Genesis 43:13–14 (id: 12835)

Jacob finally trusted *God Almighty* with his family's future. This divine title emphasizes God's power (see also [17:1](https://ref.ly/Gen17:1); [28:3](https://ref.ly/Gen28:3); [35:11](https://ref.ly/Gen35:11); [48:3](https://ref.ly/Gen48:3); [1 Kings 19:10](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:10), [14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:14); [Revelation 21:22](https://ref.ly/Rev21:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:13-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 43:16 (id: 12836)

*When Joseph saw Benjamin with his brothers:* Joseph knew Benjamin was well (see study note on 42:1–44:34). The feast was both a celebration and a test.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:16
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 42:1-44.34 (ID: `12814@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 43:18 (id: 12837)

*the brothers were frightened:* Compare [32:6–12](https://ref.ly/Gen32:6-Gen32:12). Their guilt stopped them from seeing that something good might happen.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:18

## Genesis 43:19–22 (id: 12838)

The brothers, completely vulnerable and feeling the weight of God’s judgment on their consciences, approached the manager in desperation.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:19-22

Joseph stopped being harsh to his brothers and treated them kindly instead.

## Genesis 43:26 (id: 12840)

For the second time, the brothers *bowed to the ground before him.*This fulfilled his first dream ([37:7](https://ref.ly/Gen37:7); see [42:6–7](https://ref.ly/Gen42:6-Gen42:7); compare [33:3](https://ref.ly/Gen33:3)).

## Genesis 43:29 (id: 12841)

*May God be gracious to you, my son:* Joseph’s blessing to his full brother fulfilled Jacob’s prayer ([43:14](https://ref.ly/Gen43:14)).

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 43:30 (id: 12842)

* *he was moved to tears for his brother:* Compare [42:24](https://ref.ly/Gen42:24). Joseph cried because of painful memories and lost years with his brother. He also felt joy and gratitude for seeing Benjamin again.
* *he went to a private room:* This was because he was not ready to reveal his identity.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 43:33 (id: 12843)

*in astonishment:* This ruler knew more about the brothers than seemed possible (compare [44:15](https://ref.ly/Gen44:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:33


## Genesis 43:34 (id: 12844)

The brothers experienced generous and gracious treatment from God through Joseph.

Joseph tested them. He showed special favor to Benjamin, who was also Rachel’s son. In the past, their father’s favoritism toward Joseph had caused jealousy and hatred.

By treating Benjamin with favor, Joseph tested whether they would respond with the same anger as before. This situation revealed whether their attitudes and behavior had changed.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 44:1–34 (id: 12846)

The brothers seemed different. They felt sorry for what they did to Joseph. They showed honor by returning the money and by bringing Benjamin. Given a chance to get rid of Rachel’s other son, Benjamin, would they do it?

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:1-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Exempt (ID: `keyterm:Exempt`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`)

## Genesis 44:2 (id: 12852)

Joseph was giving his brothers the chance to abandon Benjamin if they wanted to. Joseph was testing them to see if they were loyal to the family and faithful to their father.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`)

## Genesis 44:5 (id: 12854)

* This description would help the brothers see that the ruler knew things that other people could not know.
* *Hydromancy* (trying to learn hidden things by pouring water into oil) and *oenomancy* (trying to learn hidden things by pouring wine into other liquids) were forms of divination (seeking knowledge through supernatural means) used in the ancient Near East. A special cup was often used for these practices.

    Joseph was still pretending (see [42:7](https://ref.ly/Gen42:7); compare [30:27](https://ref.ly/Gen30:27); see also [Leviticus 19:26](https://ref.ly/Lev19:26); [Numbers 23:23](https://ref.ly/Num23:23); [Deuteronomy 18:10–11](https://ref.ly/Deut18:10-Deut18:11)). He knew that only God reveals the future (see [Genesis 37:5–9](https://ref.ly/Gen37:5-Gen37:9); [40:8](https://ref.ly/Gen40:8); [41:16](https://ref.ly/Gen41:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:5


## Genesis 44:9–10 (id: 12855)

The brothers suggested a punishment that was too harsh. In ancient times, people often risked their lives based on their words. The palace manager agreed but imposed a punishment that matched Joseph's intentions ([44:17](https://ref.ly/Gen44:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Exempt (ID: `keyterm:Exempt`)

## Genesis 44:11–12 (id: 12847)

The palace manager knew where the cup was, but he created more anxiety by beginning with the oldest.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 44:13 (id: 12848)

*they all tore their clothes:* They knew what it would do to Jacob to lose Benjamin ([42:38](https://ref.ly/Gen42:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

## Genesis 44:14 (id: 12849)

This time, the brothers did not bow politely (see [42:6–7](https://ref.ly/Gen42:6-Gen42:7); [43:26](https://ref.ly/Gen43:26)). They fell to the ground in desperation, fulfilling Joseph’s first dream for the third time (see [37:10](https://ref.ly/Gen37:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 44:16 (id: 12850)

* Judah again spoke for the group.
* *God has exposed the iniquity of your servants:* God was completing the work of repentance in their hearts. Judah again proposed that they all be punished. Benjamin was seemingly guilty of this theft, but all of the others were guilty of sin against Joseph. They preferred not returning to Jacob at all versus seeing his grief at the loss of Benjamin ([44:34](https://ref.ly/Gen44:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Genesis 44:18–34 (id: 12851)

* Judah kept his promise to make sure Benjamin was safe ([43:8–10](https://ref.ly/Gen43:8-Gen43:10)). His lengthy plea to take Benjamin's place in prison is one of the most sincere examples of intercession in the Bible ([44:33](https://ref.ly/Gen44:33)). It showed Judah's care for their father and his willingness to sacrifice everything for his brother. With this integrity, Judah proved himself a true leader, worthy of the firstborn's blessing (see [John 15:13](https://ref.ly/John15:13)). Through him, Israel's kings would emerge (see [Genesis 49:10](https://ref.ly/Gen49:10)).
* The brothers had fully repented, as shown by Judah’s intercession. Because of their change, Joseph could reveal himself to them ([45:1–15](https://ref.ly/Gen45:1-Gen45:15)). He arranged for the family to join him in Egypt, where there was food ([45:16](https://ref.ly/Gen45:16); [47:12](https://ref.ly/Gen47:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:18-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Genesis 44:32–34 (id: 12853)

Judah was willing to give up his family, his future, and his freedom for others.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:32-34


## Genesis 45:1–15 (id: 12856)

In one of the book's most dramatic scenes, Joseph told his brothers who he was. This brought the process of reconciling with them to a climax (see also [50:14–21](https://ref.ly/Gen50:14-Gen50:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:1-15

## Genesis 45:2 (id: 12864)

This is the third time Joseph cried over his brothers in the story (see also [42:24](https://ref.ly/Gen42:24); [43:30](https://ref.ly/Gen43:30); [45:14](https://ref.ly/Gen45:14); [50:17](https://ref.ly/Gen50:17); compare [50:1](https://ref.ly/Gen50:1)).

## Genesis 45:3 (id: 12865)

The news shocked the brothers. They were unable to speak because of amazement and fear ([45:5](https://ref.ly/Gen45:5); compare [50:15](https://ref.ly/Gen50:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 45:5–8 (id: 12866)

*it was not you who sent me here, but God*: This is the main message of Jacob's family story ([37:2](https://ref.ly/Gen37:2)). As the Lord had told Abraham, he was bringing the Israelites into Egypt ([15:13](https://ref.ly/Gen15:13)). God sent Joseph ahead to prepare a way to save his family during the famine.

This is a clear statement about God’s sovereignty (his complete rule and control over all things). Joseph explained that God was at work through all events and human actions to carry out his plan.

Because God is in control, people can forgive and be restored to those who have hurt them. If people do not believe that God rules over all, they may blame others and try to get even. But those who trust God believe that he is at work, even through human evil (see also [Romans 8:28–30](https://ref.ly/Rom8:28-Rom8:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:5-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 45:9–13 (id: 12867)

Joseph told his brothers to inform Jacob. The entire family needed to move to Egypt and live in Goshen, a fertile area in the Nile delta (see [47:1–12](https://ref.ly/Gen47:1-Gen47:12)). If they did not come to Egypt, they would not survive the upcoming five years of famine.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:9-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Be Poor (ID: `keyterm:Be`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 45:10 (id: 12857)

The region of Goshen was in the northeast corner of Egypt, a few days' walk from Canaan. Jacob's family stayed there because it had food and water for them and their animals. Later, the Egyptians forced them into slave labor there.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 45:14–15 (id: 12858)

Joseph was reunited with his brothers, first with Benjamin and then with the rest. Their previous hatred and jealousy of Joseph ([37:4–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:4-Gen37:11)) had come to an end. The brothers experienced forgiveness from God and from Joseph. Instead of commanding his brothers to bow down to him (see [42:6–7](https://ref.ly/Gen42:6-Gen42:7)), Joseph welcomed them. Joseph held no grudge because he accepted what had happened as God’s work and saw the good that had resulted (see [50:14–21](https://ref.ly/Gen50:14-Gen50:21)). 

This is how wisdom rules: The wise leader will forgive and restore (see study note on 37:2–50:26).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:14-15
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 37:2-50.26 (ID: `12724@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 45:16–25 (id: 12859)

Joseph instructed his brothers to bring Jacob and the whole family to Egypt. Grateful for Joseph saving Egypt ([45:18](https://ref.ly/Gen45:18); see [47:20](https://ref.ly/Gen47:20)), Pharaoh promised them the best land in Egypt. Joseph also gave them Egypt's finest products.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:16-25

## Genesis 45:24 (id: 12861)

*Do not quarrel on the way!:* When they were away from Joseph, they might begin to accuse one another about the past or argue about how to explain what had happened to Jacob. It was now time to put the past behind them and enjoy the reunion.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:24


## Genesis 45:26–28 (id: 12862)

As expected, Jacob was shocked when he heard that his son Joseph was still alive and ruling all Egypt. But as he heard their full report and saw everything Joseph had sent, he became convinced it was true. At once, he prepared to go to Egypt and see Joseph again after 22 years.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:26-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Wagon (ID: `realia:Wagon`)

## Genesis 45:27 (id: 12863)

*Jacob was revived**:* This royal invitation was a turning point for Jacob and his sons. Jacob was old and had little hope left. The 10 brothers were weighed down with guilt and fear.

This moment also fulfilled what God had said earlier ([15:13–16](https://ref.ly/Gen15:13-Gen15:16)): his people would live in a foreign land for a time and there become a great nation, while remaining a distinct people.

The joyful news about Joseph changed the lives of this whole family for the better.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Wagon (ID: `realia:Wagon`)

## Genesis 46:1 (id: 12869)

* *set out:* A little over 200 years earlier, Abraham also went to Egypt during a famine in Canaan ([12:10](https://ref.ly/Gen12:10)), and God protected him there.

This was after resolving land and water rights with the Philistines ([21:31–33](https://ref.ly/Gen21:31-Gen21:33)). Isaac had lived there, and Jacob had lived there before fleeing from Esau’s anger ([28:10](https://ref.ly/Gen28:10)).

## Genesis 46:26 (id: 12872)

*numbered sixty\-six persons:* This includes those who traveled with Jacob to Egypt. But it does not include his sons' wives, the servants, or others in the household. It also does not include Joseph, Ephraim, Manasseh, and Jacob.

## Genesis 46:27 (id: 12873)

seventy: This includes Joseph, Ephraim, Manasseh, and Jacob. *Seventy* is a symbolic number for perfection or completion (see “Symbolic Numbers” Theme Note). From these *seventy* (meaning all of Israel), the nation of Israel would grow and bless the *seventy* nations (meaning all the nations) of the world (see study note on Genesis 10:2–32; compare [12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)).

## Genesis 46:30 (id: 12876)

Jacob was happy to see his beloved son alive. This son was the firstborn of his chosen wife Rachel and the designated family leader (see study note on 48:5–7; see also [1 Chronicles 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)). This was more than a family reunion. It confirmed that God's plan was still in place.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 46:30
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 48:5-7 (ID: `12895@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 46:34 (id: 12877)

The Canaanites would have mixed with the Israelites if they stayed in Canaan. But, Egyptians disliked Semitic shepherds due to ethnic pride and kept them separate (see [43:32](https://ref.ly/Gen43:32)). When Jacob’s family settled in Egypt, this separation helped them increase into a large nation while keeping their identity.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 46:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 47:1 (id: 12882)

Ancient Egyptian texts do not mention Goshen (see study note on 45:10). Later Egyptian writings called it "the district of Rameses" ([47:11](https://ref.ly/Gen47:11); see [Exodus 1:11](https://ref.ly/Exod1:11)). It was fertile and near Joseph's court. This suggests it was on the eastern side of the Nile delta.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:1
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 45:10 (ID: `12857@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Genesis 47:1–6 (id: 12880)

Pharaoh responded as Joseph hoped by giving Jacob’s family the best part of the land; he even gave some of the brothers oversight of his own livestock ([47:6](https://ref.ly/Gen47:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Genesis 47:7–10 (id: 12887)

When Jacob entered and left Pharaoh’s court, he blessed Pharaoh. This showed Jacob's role as God's representative (see [Hebrews 7:7](https://ref.ly/Heb7:7)). God promised to bless those who blessed Abraham’s family ([Genesis 12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2-Gen12:3)), so Jacob blessed Pharaoh and Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:7-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 47:13–26 (id: 12881)

The Lord blessed Pharaoh because Pharaoh was blessing Abraham’s descendants ([12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)). Through Joseph’s smart management in Egypt, the Lord saved the people from hunger and helped Pharaoh prosper. During the famine, Joseph sold food to the Egyptians, accepting money, livestock, and eventually land as payment. Then Pharaoh owned almost all of Egypt. Meanwhile, God gave Israel some of the best land in Egypt where they could live, work, and multiply.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:13-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 47:21 (id: 12883)

*reduced the people to servitude:*In Hebrew script, the words for *slaves* (Hebrew *‘abadim*) and *towns* (Hebrew *‘arim*) look very similar. Moving the people into the towns does not fit the context well, so most translations choose *slaves* as the reading that makes the most sense (compare [47:20](https://ref.ly/Gen47:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Genesis 47:27 (id: 12884)

God blessed his people, just as he had promised Abraham. He said that Abraham’s descendants would be too many to count ([15:5](https://ref.ly/Gen15:5); [22:17](https://ref.ly/Gen22:17)). But they still had to wait for the second part of the promise, that they would own the land of Canaan ([17:8](https://ref.ly/Gen17:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 47:29–31 (id: 12885)

* *bury me with them:* Compare [49:29–33](https://ref.ly/Gen49:29-Gen49:33). Jacob wanted to be buried with Abraham and Isaac in the cave of Machpelah (see study note on 49:29–33; see [23:1–20](https://ref.ly/Gen23:1-Gen23:20); [25:7–10](https://ref.ly/Gen25:7-Gen25:10); [35:27–29](https://ref.ly/Gen35:27-Gen35:29)).
* *put your hand under my thigh**:* This custom was a serious oath to continue the covenant (compare with [24:1–9](https://ref.ly/Gen24:1-Gen24:9) and study note on 24:2). The covenant's main promise was descendants in the promised land that would be too many to count.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:29-31
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 24:2 (ID: `12490@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Genesis 49:29-33 (ID: `12913@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Genesis 47:31 (id: 12886)

After taking the oath, Jacob bowed in worship and thanked the Lord for making sure he would be buried with his ancestors in the promised land (compare [1 Kings 1:47](https://ref.ly/1Kgs1:47)).

## Genesis 48:1–22 (id: 12888)

When Jacob blessed Ephraim and Manasseh, he showed faith in God's promise to continue. He understood that God's ways differ from human ways. From Jacob's long life, the writer of Hebrews chose the blessing of Joseph's sons as his greatest act of faith ([Hebrews 11:21](https://ref.ly/Heb11:21)). Jacob followed God's will by giving the main blessing to the younger son instead of the older one, without any trickery or negative outcomes.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:1-22

Jacob remembered how God Almighty appeared to him and promised him Abraham's blessing: countless descendants living in the land … *as an everlasting possession* (compare [28:10–22](https://ref.ly/Gen28:10-Gen28:22)).

## Genesis 48:5–7 (id: 12895)

Jacob remembered Rachel (see [35:16–20](https://ref.ly/Gen35:16-Gen35:20)) and blessed Joseph by making his two sons equal heirs with his other sons. The tribes of Ephraim and Manasseh would share equally with Jacob's other tribes (see [Joshua 16–17](https://ref.ly/Josh16:1-Josh17:18)). Jacob also gave Ephraim, Joseph’s younger son, the birthright (see [1 Chronicles 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)). Because of this blessing, Ephraim and Manasseh became large and powerful tribes (see [Joshua 17:14–18](https://ref.ly/Josh17:14-Josh17:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:5-7

## Genesis 48:10 (id: 12889)

Like his father Isaac, Jacob now gave the blessing when his eyesight was failing (compare [27:1](https://ref.ly/Gen27:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 48:14 (id: 12890)

The right hand was for the head of the firstborn, and Jacob was deliberately giving that position to the younger son. That pattern was followed for four consecutive generations: 

* Isaac over Ishmael,
* Jacob over Esau,
* Joseph over Reuben, and
* Ephraim over Manasseh.

Many years later, Ephraim became the leading tribe in the northern kingdom, superior to the tribe of Manasseh. The entire northern kingdom of Israel was occasionally called Ephraim (see study notes on 2 Chronicles 28:12; Isaiah 11:13; Ezekiel 37:15–28; Hosea 6:4; Zechariah 9:10).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:14
* **Associated Articles:** Ezekiel 37:15-28 (ID: `11764@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Hosea 6:4 (ID: `13462@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Isaiah 11:13 (ID: `15841@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Zechariah 9:10 (ID: `26133@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`)

## Genesis 48:15–16 (id: 12891)

When Jacob blessed Joseph, he used three titles to describe the God he trusted: 

1. the God who was in covenant with his fathers Abraham and Isaac ([28:13](https://ref.ly/Gen28:13); [31:5](https://ref.ly/Gen31:5), [42](https://ref.ly/Gen31:42); [32:9](https://ref.ly/Gen32:9); [46:3](https://ref.ly/Gen46:3));
2. the God who had been his shepherd (one who guides and cares for his people; see [49:24](https://ref.ly/Gen49:24); [Exodus 6:6](https://ref.ly/Exod6:6); [Psalm 23:1](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1); [Isaiah 59:20](https://ref.ly/Isa59:20)); and
3. the angel (a messenger from God) who had rescued him from all harm.

Jacob prayed the same blessings for Joseph’s sons.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:15-16

*Not so, my father!:* Joseph was upset because he expected God to act in a predictable way. But faith understands that God's ways and thoughts are different from human ones. Jacob spent his life learning this lesson, but he eventually understood and acted on it.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:17-19

## Genesis 49:1–2 (id: 12897)

Jacob's words were carefully chosen prophecies. *The days to come* refer to the conquest and settlement of the promised land and the future messianic age. All tribes would share in the blessing and enter the land with Joshua. But they would not all participate in the same way.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 49:1–28 (id: 12896)

By faith, and as one who speaks for God, Jacob looked ahead to Israel’s future in the land and beyond to a greater future still.

At the end of the time of the patriarchs (the early fathers of Israel), he spoke about what would happen to each tribe. He did this as he considered each of his sons, one by one, just as Noah had done earlier (compare [9:25–27](https://ref.ly/Gen9:25-Gen9:27)). 

The character and actions of each ancestor affected the lives of their descendants ([Exodus 20:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod20:5-Exod20:6); [34:6–7](https://ref.ly/Exod34:6-Exod34:7); [Numbers 14:18](https://ref.ly/Num14:18); [Jeremiah 32:18](https://ref.ly/Jer32:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:1-28

## Genesis 49:3–4 (id: 12914)

*Uncontrolled as the waters*: As the firstborn, Reuben should have led the family. But he acted without control, like water that boils or flows wildly (for example, [35:22](https://ref.ly/Gen35:22)). Because of this, Jacob said that Reuben would not succeed as a leader (see [37:21–22](https://ref.ly/Gen37:21-Gen37:22), [29](https://ref.ly/Gen37:29); [42:22](https://ref.ly/Gen42:22), [37–38](https://ref.ly/Gen42:37-Gen42:38); compare [Judges 5:15–16](https://ref.ly/Judg5:15-Judg5:16); [1 Chronicles 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:3-4

## Genesis 49:5–7 (id: 12916)

* Simeon and Levi were violent and lawless. Instead of seeking justice, they allowed their uncontrolled anger to control them and ignored the value of life ([34:24–29](https://ref.ly/Gen34:24-Gen34:29)).
* *I will disperse them:* Simeon's land mostly became part of Judah's ([Joshua 19:1](https://ref.ly/Josh19:1), [9](https://ref.ly/Josh19:9)). Levi was given a more honorable future because the Levites became the priestly tribe (see [Exodus 32:25–29](https://ref.ly/Exod32:25-Exod32:29)). But they did not have their own region ([Joshua 21](https://ref.ly/Josh21:1-Josh21:45)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:5-7

## Genesis 49:8–12 (id: 12917)

The blessing for Judah is the most notable. In this message, Jacob said that Judah would be strong like a lion and would defeat his enemies. His brothers would also honor and praise him (compare [29:35](https://ref.ly/Gen29:35); see, for example, [Ruth 4:11–12](https://ref.ly/Ruth4:11-Ruth4:12); [1 Samuel 18:6–7](https://ref.ly/1Sam18:6-1Sam18:7); [Psalms 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12), [45](https://ref.ly/Ps45:1-Ps45:17), [72](https://ref.ly/Ps72:1-Ps72:20); [Isaiah 11:1–13](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:13)).

## Genesis 49:10 (id: 12898)

* This verse anticipates the kingship in Judah (compare [17:6](https://ref.ly/Gen17:6), [16](https://ref.ly/Gen17:16); [35:11](https://ref.ly/Gen35:11)). Although Joseph received the birthright blessing, Judah would provide Israel's rulers (see [1 Chronicles 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)). Many kings from Judah would hold the *scepter*, the symbol of rule. The final king would be the one to whom the scepter belongs, the promised Messiah (see [2 Samuel 7:4–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:4-2Sam7:16); [Psalms 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12), [45](https://ref.ly/Ps45:1-Ps45:17), [60](https://ref.ly/Ps60:1-Ps60:12); [Isaiah 11](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:16); [Ezekiel 21:26–27](https://ref.ly/Ezek21:26-Ezek21:27); [Zechariah 9:9](https://ref.ly/Zech9:9); [Revelation 5:5](https://ref.ly/Rev5:5)).
* *from between his feet:* This is a poetic way to refer to reproductive organs.
* *until Shiloh comes and the allegiance of the nations is his*: This wording reflects a long\-standing way of understanding a difficult Hebrew phrase. Other translations include *until the coming of the one to whom it belongs* or *until tribute is brought to him and the peoples obey*.

    The right to rule Israel belongs to Judah’s descendant through David’s line ([2 Samuel 7:8–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:8-2Sam7:16)). He will eventually rule all nations, as shown by the bringing of tribute (see [Psalms 68:29](https://ref.ly/Ps68:29); [72:8–11](https://ref.ly/Ps72:8-Ps72:11); [Isaiah 2:2–4](https://ref.ly/Isa2:2-Isa2:4); [Ephesians 4:8–10](https://ref.ly/Eph4:8-Eph4:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shiloh (ID: `person:Shiloh`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Staff (ID: `realia:Staff`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`)

## Genesis 49:11–12 (id: 12899)

* These descriptions describe the abundance of the Messiah’s kingdom (see [Isaiah 61:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa61:6-Isa61:7); [65:21–25](https://ref.ly/Isa65:21-Isa65:25); [Zechariah 3:10](https://ref.ly/Zech3:10)). When the Messiah arrives, the earth will be like paradise, full of beauty and abundance.
* *He ties his donkey to the vine:* Grapevines will be so plentiful that people will use them as hitching posts, and wine will be as plentiful as fresh water (see [Amos 9:13–14](https://ref.ly/Amos9:13-Amos9:14); [Zechariah 3:10](https://ref.ly/Zech3:10)).
* *His eyes are darker than wine, and his teeth are whiter than milk**:* He will be strong and healthy, as will be the time of his rule. The miracle of turning water into wine was the first sign from Jesus that the Messiah had arrived ([John 2:1–12](https://ref.ly/John2:1-John2:12)). It was a preview of even better things to come.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

## Genesis 49:13 (id: 12900)

The message said Zebulun would live by the sea and be a safe harbor. But when the land was divided, the tribe settled inland instead (see [Joshua 19:10–16](https://ref.ly/Josh19:10-Josh19:16)). The message did not give specific borders for the tribes.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:13

Issachar would become a tribe that had to do hard work for others. Invading armies often controlled Issachar.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:14-15

Dan (meaning "judge") was meant to bring justice. But the tribe chose betrayal, like a snake by the road, striking in a dangerous and unexpected way (see [Judges 18](https://ref.ly/Judg18:1-Judg18:31)).

## Genesis 49:18 (id: 12903)

At this point, Jacob paused to speak words of hope. He may have been reminding his sons of their need to depend on the Lord. Or he may have been expressing hope in the Messiah’s reign, when he and his descendants would be rescued from all trouble, grief, and human betrayal.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Genesis 49:19 (id: 12904)

Three of the six Hebrew words in this verse are wordplays on the name *Gad*, which means "attack." Gad will be attacked by raiding groups, but he will also attack them back. The tribes living east of the Jordan River often experienced raids on their borders (see [Joshua 13](https://ref.ly/Josh13:1-Josh13:33); [2 Kings 10:32–33](https://ref.ly/2Kgs10:32-2Kgs10:33); [1 Chronicles 5:18–19](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:18-1Chr5:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gad (ID: `person:Gad`)

## Genesis 49:20 (id: 12905)

Asher would be fertile and productive, providing rich foods. The tribe settled along the rich northern coast of Canaan.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Asher (ID: `person:Asher`)

## Genesis 49:21 (id: 12906)

Naphtali was like a doe. This may mean the tribe would live as a free people in the mountains (compare [Judges 5:18](https://ref.ly/Judg5:18)). The tribe of Naphtali settled in the hill country northwest of the Sea of Galilee.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Naphtali (ID: `person:Naphtali`); Deer (ID: `fauna:Deer`)

## Genesis 49:22–26 (id: 12907)

This message about Joseph is longer and more detailed than the others, because it contains the main blessing(see [1 Chronicles 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)). Jacob gave Joseph many promises of victory and success.

Some from the tribe of Ephraim who later won victories in battle include Joshua ([Joshua 6](https://ref.ly/Josh6:1-Josh6:27), [8](https://ref.ly/Josh8:1-Josh8:35), [10](https://ref.ly/Josh10:1-Josh10:43), [12](https://ref.ly/Josh12:1-Josh12:24)) and Deborah ([Judges 4](https://ref.ly/Judg4:1-Judg4:24)). From the tribe of Manasseh, Gideon ([Judges 6–8](https://ref.ly/Judg6:1-Judg8:35)) and Jephthah ([Judges 11:1–12:7](https://ref.ly/Judg11:1-Judg12:7)) were also victorious leaders.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:22-26
