## Genesis 49:24–26 (id: 12908)

*the Mighty One of Jacob … the Shepherd …the Rock of Israel … the God of your father … the Almighty*: Five names for God introduce five blessings. God is the giver of all good things.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:24-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 49:25 (id: 12909)

* The *blessings from the heavens* brought rain for crops.
* The *blessings of the depths below* provided streams and wells.
* The *blessings of the* *breasts and womb* led to many children.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Genesis 49:26 (id: 12910)

*Joseph …* *the prince of his brothers:* He is a leader among his brothers, showing his strong character and high status.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 49:27 (id: 12911)

The oracle about Benjamin describes a violent tribe (see [Judges 20](https://ref.ly/Judg20:1-Judg20:48); [1 Samuel 9:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Sam9:1-1Sam9:2); [19:10](https://ref.ly/1Sam19:10); [22:17](https://ref.ly/1Sam22:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Wolf (ID: `fauna:Wolf`)

## Genesis 49:28 (id: 12912)

These prophecies cover a wide range, predicting the future of various tribes in general terms. Individuals could receive great blessings from God through faith and obedience, no matter what happened to their clan.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 49:29–33 (id: 12913)

*Bury me with my fathers:* This grave in the land of Canaan represented hope for the future (compare [47:29–30](https://ref.ly/Gen47:29-Gen47:30)). Others buried at the cave of Machpelah near Hebron were:

* Sarah ([23:19](https://ref.ly/Gen23:19)),
* Abraham ([25:7–9](https://ref.ly/Gen25:7-Gen25:9)),
* Isaac ([35:27–29](https://ref.ly/Gen35:27-Gen35:29)),
* Rebekah, and
* Leah.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:29-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Ephron (ID: `person:Ephron.2`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Machpelah (ID: `place:Machpelah`); Leah (ID: `person:Leah`); Rebekah (ID: `person:Rebekah`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Mamre (ID: `place:Mamre`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Genesis 49:33 (id: 12915)

Jacob died at the age of 147 ([47:28](https://ref.ly/Gen47:28)), bringing his life of struggle and sorrow to an end. Jacob had always had a strong desire for God's blessing. He had a deep devotion and consistently relied on God no matter what happened. 

In the end, he died with genuine faith. He learned where true blessings come from. Through his faith, he would be able to pass these blessings on to his sons ([Hebrews 11:21](https://ref.ly/Heb11:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Genesis 50:1–6 (id: 12929)

Like his father and his grandfather, Jacob’s death marked the end of an important time in Israel’s history.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 50:2 (id: 12937)

Jacob’s body was embalmed for burial in typical Egyptian fashion. Embalming is the process of preserving a body after death.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 50:3 (id: 12938)

The Egyptians mourned Jacob for 70 days, just two days less than they would for a pharaoh. This showed their great respect for Joseph.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Genesis 50:4–6 (id: 12939)

Joseph needed Pharaoh’s permission to leave his official duties for a short time. He wanted to bury his father in Canaan.

Pharaoh quickly gave permission to Joseph, who had once been a slave.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 50:7–9 (id: 12940)

This was Joseph’s first return to his homeland in 39 years. The trip was temporary. Hundreds of years later, the family of Israel would permanently leave Egypt, taking Joseph's bones with them for burial in the promised land (see [50:25](https://ref.ly/Gen50:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:7-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 50:10–13 (id: 12930)

This journey to Canaan was a sorrowful one, because they went there to bury a man. The next journey into the land would be to live there.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:10-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Atad (ID: `place:Atad`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Machpelah (ID: `place:Machpelah`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Ephron (ID: `person:Ephron.2`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Mamre (ID: `place:Mamre`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 50:15–18 (id: 12931)

The brothers begged Joseph to forgive them, referring to themselves as his slaves (compare [37:7](https://ref.ly/Gen37:7); [44:16](https://ref.ly/Gen44:16), [33](https://ref.ly/Gen44:33)). They worried that Joseph had only made peace with them earlier because he wanted to see his father again. Now that Jacob and Pharaoh were not there to restrain him, they feared he might seek revenge. But *Joseph wept* because they still feared he would punish them.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:15-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Genesis 50:19–21 (id: 12932)

Joseph comforted his brothers, explaining that God intended to fulfill the promised blessing (compare [45:5](https://ref.ly/Gen45:5), [7–9](https://ref.ly/Gen45:7-Gen45:9)). He also promised them kindness and support (see [45:11](https://ref.ly/Gen45:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:19-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 50:22–23 (id: 12933)

Joseph lived to see his great\-great\-grandchildren by Ephraim, and his great\-grandchildren by Manasseh. This was a sign of God's blessing (see [Psalm 128:6](https://ref.ly/Ps128:6); [Proverbs 17:6](https://ref.ly/Prov17:6); [Isaiah 53:10](https://ref.ly/Isa53:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:22-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Machir (ID: `person:Machir`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Genesis 50:23 (id: 12934)

*were brought up on Joseph’s knees**:* Placing them on his knees at their birth was a symbolic act signifying that they came from him and belonged to him (compare [Job 3:12](https://ref.ly/Job3:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Machir (ID: `person:Machir`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`)

## Genesis 50:24–25 (id: 12935)

* *God will surely visit you:* These words from Joseph are repeated twice. They summarize the hope found in both the Old Testament and the New Testament. God would come to his people and act to save them.

    God’s coming in the Messiah (the promised descendant of Abraham and God’s chosen king) would bring an end to the curse (the result of sin) and bring God’s promised blessing in a new creation (a renewed world). God’s faithful people would wait with hope for this to happen.

* Like his father, Joseph made his brothers promise to take his bones out of Egypt when God came to lead them back to Canaan ("attend to you"; see [Exodus 13:19](https://ref.ly/Exod13:19); [Joshua 24:32](https://ref.ly/Josh24:32); [Hebrews 11:22](https://ref.ly/Heb11:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:24-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`)

## Genesis 50:26 (id: 12936)

* Joseph’s death signified the end of his generation (see study notes on 25:7–8; 35:1–29) and of the patriarchal age. From this point forward, God dealt with Israel as a nation. •
* Joseph’s body stayed in Egypt as a symbol of hope for slaves awaiting the promised land (see [Exodus 13:19](https://ref.ly/Exod13:19); [Hebrews 11:39–40](https://ref.ly/Heb11:39-Heb11:40)). They eventually buried him in Shechem (see [Joshua 24:32](https://ref.ly/Josh24:32)), where Jacob had originally sent him ([Genesis 37:13](https://ref.ly/Gen37:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:26
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 25:7-8 (ID: `12527@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Genesis 35:1-29 (ID: `12690@TyndaleStudyNotes`)
